#5212. Coinbase Pitches a Full Token Lifecycle Service, From Public Sales to Custody

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: Coinbase’s November 5 product update describes an integrated set of services for token issuers, from sales and distribution through listings, custody and liquidity. It cites Monad, Ethena and Hyperliquid as examples, but the examples are company-selected and do not eliminate the risks or regulatory limits facing token projects.

For a blockchain project, issuing a token may be the relatively easy part. Finding real users, managing custody, listing on exchanges and supporting institutional trading each introduce complicated operational decisions. In a November 5 announcement, Coinbase presented those tasks as parts of one integrated platform for token issuers.

The pitch is straightforward: use fewer separate service providers and reduce the number of operational handoffs. For developers and investors, the interesting question is how much of that convenience comes from genuine technical integration and how much reflects the commercial advantage of a large incumbent bundling its own services.

Coinbase pointed to a public MON token sale involving an affiliate of Monad Foundation, describing more than 85,000 purchasers in over 70 countries. It said the token now has more than 150,000 unique traders and $7.2 billion in spot volume on its platform. Those are company-reported measures from a chosen example, not a representative result for every issuer that uses similar services.

With Ethena, Coinbase described a relationship that expanded from token-management tools into institutional custody, wallets and trading. It also cited a USDC-related product and hundreds of millions of dollars in new deposits. For Hyperliquid, the company emphasised its role in deploying USDC treasury infrastructure and supporting large transfers.

The three examples involve different business models, market structures and needs. They demonstrate possible uses for the platform, but readers should not assume that success in one project translates directly into another token’s demand or risk profile.

Bundling services can remove duplicate work for a project team. One integration may simplify onboarding, compliance coordination and support. It can also make changing suppliers harder if critical functions become dependent on the same provider.

A token issuer should examine who legally controls assets at every stage, what happens if a service is paused, how withdrawal requests are handled and whether exchanges and custodians have the right safeguards for each market. Convenience is not a substitute for operational resilience.

That issue connects to NewsBTC’s earlier coverage of Coinbase’s restrictions on certain stablecoins for EEA customers. An exchange service can be available in one region but restricted in another without changing the underlying token network. Eligibility and local regulation remain part of any credible launch plan.

Coinbase also said it is broadening work around tokenised equities and access to private markets. Those plans should be distinguished from products already live and from securities that may require additional identity or jurisdictional checks.

The practical questions go beyond transaction volume: what are the total fees, the terms of custody, the geographic eligibility limits, support commitments and data-portability arrangements? What functions can an issuer replace if its needs change? Where do responsibilities sit when an incident affects users across several services?

NewsBTC recently examined the operational requirements of tokenised securities venues, where ownership records and corporate actions remain essential despite the appeal of blockchain settlement.

Coinbase’s new presentation shows how major crypto platforms are positioning themselves as end-to-end service providers rather than standalone exchanges. Whether that gives issuers better outcomes will depend on transparency, competition and the performance of the services after the marketing presentation ends.

#5211. Google Tells Schools They Can Reject AI—But They'll Lose Search, Too

Google Tells Schools They Can Reject AI—But They'll Lose Search, Too

Google is giving schools more freedom to reject artificial intelligence—provided they're also willing to give up Google Search, which is arguably what everybody in the world uses Google for.

The company announced Tuesday that its AI products will be disabled by default for K-12 student accounts unless administrators explicitly enable them. But schools that want to ban AI entirely will also have to disable Google's search engine, a tool millions of students rely on for everyday research.

Chris Phillips, Google's vice president and general manager of education, tried to justify the reasoning during a meeting with educators.

“Search and AI are deeply integrated,” he said according to Education Week. Schools uncomfortable with AI can choose not to enable Search at all.

Google says administrators will be able to activate or disable Gemini, Gemini Notebook, Gemini in Classroom, and Search individually. That means schools can block the standalone chatbots while keeping Search available, but they cannot remove AI from Search itself.

For teachers who want students to research sources without receiving AI-generated answers, the company is effectively offering two options: accept its AI-powered search or find another search engine.

The announcement is already drawing accusations that Google is using its dominance in education to push a technology many schools are still trying to understand, regulate or restrict.

Google says the changes are intended to address concerns that AI chatbots can undermine learning by giving students ready-made answers instead of encouraging them to solve problems themselves.

Beginning in early 2027, schools that enable Search will be offered versions with conventional AI features, such as AI Overviews and AI Mode, or the education-focused Guided Learning experience, according to The New York Times.

Neither option provides the traditional search experience without generative AI.

New York City Mayor Zohran Mamdani accused the company of “trying to use its monopoly power to force AI into our public schools,” according to CBS News.

Former Federal Trade Commission chair Lina Khan was equally critical, accusing Google of using its search monopoly to coerce schools into adopting AI.

The criticism comes as some of America's largest school districts are actively restricting generative AI. New York City, for example, imposed a one-year moratorium on student-facing generative AI through eighth grade, covering nearly 600,000 students, while Los Angeles has also restricted the technology.

Los Angeles officials had specifically hoped to preserve Google Search while removing AI-generated summaries. Under the newly announced controls, that won't be an option.

Google's position also arrives amid continuing antitrust scrutiny. A federal court ruled in 2024 that Google unlawfully monopolized the U.S. search market, and the company has faced separate legal challenges over its decision to integrate AI summaries into search results.

School administrators are expected to receive the new controls in early 2027. They will be able to decide which AI products students can access, and even apply different settings by grade level. A Google Search option without AI, however, is not among the choices.

That said, for schools that want to, ahem, unofficially ban AI without losing Google search, our guide may provide a quick solution. It takes 5 minutes and lasts forever.

#5210. Fidelity's Jurrien Timmer Explains Why He Still Prefers Bitcoin Over Gold

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. Fidelity's Director of Global Macro, Jurrien Timmer, is more bullish on Bitcoin than gold, even as both assets are under selling pressure amid a broader shift toward risk-off sentiment in financial markets.

Timmer said in a recent X post that he still prefers Bitcoin to gold for now, pointing to Bitcoin's relative price performance despite the selloff hitting both assets.

"I still prefer Bitcoin over gold for the time being, and its relative price is holding up even as both assets have sold off," Timmer stated in an X post.

I still prefer Bitcoin over gold for the time being, and its relative price is holding up even as both assets have sold off. https://t.co/1JKztVyqjd

His comments follow an earlier post on X where he noted the growing relationship between the two assets and traditional equity markets.

Timmer says Bitcoin and gold have been following equities more closely in recent weeks, as they joined the broader market's risk-off move.

"As for gold and Bitcoin, unfortunately they have gotten more correlated to equities lately and have joined the risk-off party in recent weeks. Both assets are 60-70% correlated to the SPX right now," Timmer wrote in a separate X post.

Timmer's comments on his preference for Bitcoin aren't far-fetched, as in mid-October 2024, he indicated that Bitcoin's new 4-year cycle bull market could be underway.

"And Bitcoin? A new 4-year cycle bull market could be underway, based on its power law patterns. There doesn't seem to be a narrative, but then again, bull markets rarely start with one," Timmer wrote.

The crypto market is showing mixed price action early Saturday, with Bitcoin price slightly down 0.08% in the last 24 hours to $75,904 as investors assessed a stronger-than-expected jobs report, which strengthened the case for another rate cut at next month's Federal Reserve policy meeting.

The unemployment rate unexpectedly fell to 4.1%. The strong showing surely puts a December rate decision in play as markets continued to price in another rate change this year. Fed funds futures were pricing in a more than 77% chance of a quarter-point move coming at the Dec. 9 meeting, up from more than 66% the day prior.

But next week's inflation numbers and the fate of the equities market could prove more important. Next week's CPI and PPI reports will likely be more important in the Fed's decision, as will whether the equity market stabilizes.

#5209. Uniswap whales are selling – Why $5.80 could be UNI’s next stop

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Uniswap [UNI] faced a 10.80% decline in Open Interest in the past 24 hours as prices slid 7% and daily trading volume ramped up by 36% as of writing. The increased selling pressure was a result of market-wide fear and also UNI-specific whale selling.

Lookonchain reported that a whale wallet had sold 1 million UNI tokens, worth $7.32 million, over the past two days. The wallet had received 20.39 million from the Uniswap Timelock wallet over a year ago, the analytics platform added.

Such whale selling accompanied a generally pessimistic short-term outlook the altcoin market has suffered in recent weeks. The altcoin market cap has shrunk by $173.74 billion, or 14.78%, over the past month.

Bitcoin’s [BTC] retracement from $87k also underlined the bearish crypto market expectations.

Crypto analyst Darkfost noted that spot selling was dominant on leading exchanges Binance and Coinbase. Midterm election results contributed to the selling, with BTC falling 5.5% on the 5th of November, when the S&P 500 and the Nasdaq shed 4.3% and 6.3%, respectively.

The 30-day spot net volume delta was at -$28 million on Binance, compared to $64.3 million at the end of August. If the $75k level fails for BTC, another 6.5% price decline to $70k was possible.

The weekly bias was bearish for now, though the swing structure was bullish. This was because, following the sweep of the $10.30 high from November 2025, UNI was forced to retrace.

The healthy reset might not be over yet. The $7.83 local high has been challenged but not overcome over the past month, indicating that a move towards $5.80 remained possible.

In the coming days, traders also need to watch the range formation between $6.47 and $7.78. This range has been in place for nearly a month. At the time of writing, the mid-range level at $7.13 was the local resistance to watch.

The $6.96-$7.37 area represented a local imbalance that is also expected to serve as a supply zone.

Overall, traders can maintain a bearish bias around $7.13-$7.40. A 4-hour session close above $7.8, and a weekly session close above the same level, would be a good sign for buyers that bullish momentum was back in the Uniswap market.

#5208. Elliptic Traces $124M in Bitcoin Swaps From Binance Hack Funds

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: A November 6 update from blockchain intelligence firm Elliptic says roughly $124 million of assets stolen in the October Binance breach had been converted into Bitcoin, including funds originally held as XRP and SOL. Onchain tracing can reveal movement, but it cannot by itself identify every person controlling those wallets or prove that all the converted funds have been cashed out.

The laundering trail from the huge October cryptocurrency theft attributed by investigators to North Korean-linked actors continues to evolve. In a November 6 update to its technical analysis, Elliptic said the value of stolen assets converted into Bitcoin had reached approximately $124 million.

The headline figure is striking, but the more useful story concerns how the funds moved, what remains traceable and why conversion into Bitcoin is not the same as spending money through the conventional financial system.

Elliptic’s investigation describes activity involving tens of thousands of wallets, multiple networks and an assortment of services. By its November 6 checkpoint, analysts had identified about 1,650 BTC held in new addresses following conversion from other stolen assets. The firm said roughly $50 million worth of XRP had begun moving largely towards Bitcoin, while stolen Solana tokens had also been swapped, bridged or mixed.

The firm presented this as an ongoing, rapidly changing investigation. An estimated figure tied to a timestamp should not be reported as a permanent total, and wallet clusters can change as further transactions occur.

Cross-chain movement complicates tracing because the deposit and payout sides of a transfer may occur on different networks. It can also make the trail harder to follow when funds pass through pools, aggregators and intermediaries. However, the public nature of blockchain transactions and the ability to connect wallet activity through known services can still help investigators reconstruct substantial portions of the flow.

Elliptic reported that approximately $2.85 billion of the original stolen assets had been frozen and about $3.11 billion remained in addresses linked to the theft at its latest checkpoint. The firm cautioned that only a negligible portion appeared to have been cashed out. Such estimates may change with later tracing, and asset values vary with market prices.

This matters because a criminal actor may hold large amounts of Bitcoin or Ether for some time before attempting to move value through brokers or exchanges. Public addresses can show coins being shuffled, but they generally cannot prove the real-world identity of each subsequent controller or the final ownership arrangement.

The distinction is especially relevant to compliance services. Screening against attributed addresses can alert businesses to potential sanctions or illicit-funds exposure, even when the funds have crossed networks. Yet address blacklists and automated risk scoring are not perfect substitutes for investigation and human judgement.

The evolving theft occurred alongside a different security incident affecting Chainalysis, another blockchain-analysis provider. The two incidents should not be conflated. Chainalysis described its own systems and credentials issue as unrelated to the Binance theft, while Elliptic’s work here concerns tracing funds from that separate exchange incident.

For operators, the lesson connects to the custody, counterparty and incident-response themes covered in NewsBTC’s reporting on digital-asset infrastructure restrictions. Protecting users involves controls over withdrawals, counterparties and incident communications — not simply watching one chain’s transaction explorer.

The next meaningful updates will concern verified freezes, attributed movements to identifiable services and evidence of actual cash-out. Elliptic’s data show the stolen funds are still moving through complex paths. They do not yet tell a complete story about who ultimately receives them or how much can be recovered.

#5207. Ethereum (ETH) Whales Buying Again?

According to the Arkham data spotted by Whale Alert, a wallet has bought $19.7 million worth of Ethereum.

The data shows that the wallet has bought 8,773.5 $ETH at $2,245. This whale previously bought 10,376 ETH at an average price of $1,626 in June, according to data from Yujin Monitor.

The whale's total Ethereum holdings currently sit at 23,095 ETH worth $51.69 million.

Arkham also spotted another fresh wallet 0x4AB7, that withdrew 9,777 $ETH worth about $22.5 million from Binance over the course of the past week.

The statistics on exchange net flow paint a somewhat different picture. On Saturday, ETH saw $18.32 million in net flow on all major exchanges.

If whales move their funds to exchanges en masse, this usually means short-term selling pressure.

Over the past week, US spot Ethereum ETFs have seen $365 million in outflows. In particular, the last two days marked a total of $193.2 million in outflows.

The main reason is the rapid decline on November 5, when Ethereum fell almost 10% after a short-lived rally.

Analyst Ali Martinez noted that ETH price has recently lost an important support level at $2,385. More selling pressure could mean a further decline toward $1,963.

Ethereum price is currently trading at $2,239, moving sideways around the local bottom. The local support sits at around the $2,200–$2,220 range.

A break above $2,300 would put the price in recovery mode, opening the way to the $2,400–$2,450 zone tested this week.

#5206. SWIFT Debuts Own XRP-Inclusive Ledger With Dozen Banks

Home » Ripple (XRP) » SWIFT Debuts Own XRP-Inclusive Ledger With Dozen Banks

Ripple’s XRP extends connections to multiple banks in SWIFT’s blockchain-adopting line-up.

The biggest European financial conglomerate’s grandiose move to release their own blockchain ledger didn’t go unnoticed. This November as planned, the Society for Worldwide Interbank Financial Telecommunication (SWIFT) debuted the pilot version with 12 banks, some of which are familiar names in the XRP holder community.

One of them, HSBC, is slated to start blockchain-powered operations. HSBC connects its own Tokenised Deposit Service (TDS) to SWIFT’s ledger. The major British bank actively employs Metaco’s Harmonize platform for institutional digital-asset custody (Metaco was acquired by Ripple in 2023). The enterprise-level acquisition strengthened that bond.

5 OF SWIFT'S 17 LEDGER BANKS ALREADY WORK WITH RIPPLE 🚨Swift's new blockchain ledger is live with 17 banks piloting 24/7 cross-border payments.Look who's on that list:🏦 HSBC🏦 Standard Chartered🏦 DBS🏦 FirstRand🏦 BNY, which holds RLUSD's reserves and now holds XRP… https://t.co/z7UOp1KBYz pic.twitter.com/nNP5Ql4vgn

The bank also has historical references to XRPL capabilities in internal materials and some past explorations, with an agenda to expand blockchain-powered business operations. Conducting business operations on Distributed Ledger Technology (DLT) based chains like Ripple’s XRP Ledger serves an advantage of instant settlement & cost-effectiveness.

Aside from them, there’s a few more in the 12-long list. Standard Chartered, another popular British bank, has been running the XRP-related tech stack since 2016. During October 2026 the traditional finance behemoth launched their institutional custody for Ripple’s RLUSD stablecoin on the XRP Ledger through their Dubai (DIFC) branch.

Also, there’s DBS and BNY (BNY Mellon). These two Ripple-tied major banks serve completely different purposes. BNY Mellon is the primary reserve custodian for RLUSD. Meanwhile, DBS is partnered with Franklin Templeton to list a tokenized money-market fund (sgBENJI) on XRP Ledger, tradeable against RLUSD on their digital exchange.

🚨 ES OFICIALSWIFT acaba de confirmar transacciones en vivo en blockchain con más de 12 bancos.Y los reportes dicen que está impulsado por $XRP.Este es el tipo de noticia que la gente lleva años esperando.El sistema antiguo finalmente está siendo reemplazado en tiempo real?… pic.twitter.com/2uJi0VGPI9

This is a distinct product from the SWIFT project. SWIFT’s blockchain ledger is compatible with multiple DLT networks, but Ripple’s XRP Ledger has a crystal-clear advantage in regulatory compliance and native traits. XRP, along with Stellar’s XLM & Hedera’s HBAR has native ISO 20022 support, the new gold standard in financial messaging.

The cross-border payments market, worth over $155 trillion in processed volume annually, powers global trade, remittances & commerce. Yet merchants admit it remains slow, costly and not too efficient when compared to blockchain-based counterparts. 70% of merchants expect all transactions to settle immediately by 2027, which aligns with SWIFT’s vision.

SWIFT just named 30+ @Ripple connected banks in its new 2026 payments framework.HSBC, Deutsche Bank, Santander, Standard Chartered, JPMorgan… the list goes on.XRP isn’t replacing SWIFT. It’s becoming the liquidity bridge inside it. https://t.co/Y59ziIog9J

How much of that multi-trillion pie Ripple’s XRP can take is yet to be determined. On the SWIFT topic, Ripple’s CEO Brad Garlinghouse predicted a 14% capture. However, Ripple’s business ventures don’t always translate into blockchain adoption, as seen in the 2025 example. Ripple Prime, the custody arm, processed over $13 trillion without the help of crypto.

#5205. ChatGPT Is Getting a GIF Maker Powered by OpenAI's Unannounced Images 2.6 Model

ChatGPT Is Getting a GIF Maker Powered by OpenAI's Unannounced Images 2.6 Model

The internet may soon have an unlimited supply of AI-generated dancing cats, courtesy of ChatGPT.

OpenAI is rolling out a feature that lets users generate animated GIFs directly inside ChatGPT, according to The Verge. The feature reportedly comes powered by its new image-generation model called Images 2.6, which OpenAI has yet to formally introduce through its usual product announcements.

Users can describe an animation in plain English or upload an existing photo and ask ChatGPT to bring it to life. Once the GIF is generated, they can adjust its playback speed, inspect individual frames, make edits, and share the finished animation.

OpenAI demonstrated the capability with a simple request: “can you make a gif of a cat dancing?” The resulting interface reportedly includes controls to change animation speed and examine the GIF frame by frame.

We tried generating GIFs using ChatGPT, but the feature was not available on our accounts at the time of testing. In our interaction, the model generates a series of frames, but not the animation.

The rollout appears to be gradual, so users may have to keep asking for their dancing cats a little longer.

The GIF maker is reportedly part of a broader Images 2.6 update that promises faster image generation. The Verge reports that OpenAI is bringing the model to ChatGPT and Codex users across web, desktop, and mobile platforms.

Developers are also getting two new API models, GPT-Image-2.6 Flare and GPT-Image-2.6 Sunburst, designed for faster generation and higher-quality results, respectively. OpenAI has not yet published a standalone announcement detailing the new model's capabilities, and we could not independently test its performance.

The update follows the September release of ChatGPT Images 2.5, which introduced sharper details, more precise editing, and generation speeds up to 50% faster than its predecessor.

In our previous testing, Images 2.5 went head-to-head with Google's Nano Banana 2 across six categories, with each model winning three. OpenAI's model delivered particularly impressive illustration quality and improved on the oversharpening problems that affected earlier versions, although it still made mistakes when rendering text.

The move into GIF generation also comes months after OpenAI shut down Sora, its standalone AI video-generation app, in March. Animated GIFs are considerably simpler than the videos Sora generated, but the new feature brings a form of motion generation directly into ChatGPT without requiring a separate application.

OpenAI has not provided a specific date for when every ChatGPT account will receive GIF generation.

#5204. NEAR Intents rejects ‘500M+ quotes from Binance hackers,’ but what’s next for the project?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

NEAR Intents has often grabbed attention from users due to its security and speeds. For instance, the protocol is in the news today after rejecting over 500 million transaction quotes linked to the Binance hackers. The amount seemed to be proceeds from 29th October’s exploit.

Simply put, the move implied that despite the infrastructure being permissionless, it does not necessitate a free pass for money laundering.

NEAR Protocol [NEAR] briefly fell from the top-20 cryptos by market cap, but it has since reclaimed its spot, rising to 18th place. At press time, the altcoin was down by over 12% on the weekly charts as capital outflows surged despite infrastructure adoption.

On the adoption front, the protocol has integrated PEARL, allowing users to deposit, swap across 180+ assets, and hold and pay with it across 35 chains. Furthermore, the chain has enabled India’s largest IPO ever, JIO, a day after launching RELIANCE.

While network adoption has continued to grow, demand has been declining. For example, Bitwise’s NEAR ETF, which saw over $88 million in inflows in the first four weeks on Wall Street, has since turned negative.

Last week, the product lost about $3.43 million in capital, while this week, it has already lost $4.38 million. Worth noting, however, that the cumulative total net inflows were $80.45 million.

Additionally, the Total Value Locked (TVL) for the entire chain fell by over $70 million in a month, from $378 million to $306 million, according to DefiLlama. For NEAR Intents’ TVL, it fell from $267 million to around $190 million.

On the price charts, NEAR has been testing the 100-day EMA at $3.73, which was in alignment with the 50% Fibonacci Retracement level. The altcoin also broke the neckline at $4.50, which hinted at a shift in the market structure.

The MACD indicator bars seemed to be fading too, indicating that bearish strength was slowly diminishing. If bears give up control, the altcoin may hold above the EMA support.

If NEAR breaks below $3.76, its price could revisit the 0.618 and 0.786 Fib levels at $3.24 and $2.49, respectively. Therefore, as the altcoin continues to make lower highs and lows on the daily chart, the ultimate test will be at $3.73.

#5203. 'Congratulations,' Peter Schiff Reacts as Saylor's Bitcoin Treasury Sits at Cost Basis

Long-time Bitcoin critic and pro-gold advocate Peter Schiff has stirred discussions across the crypto ecosystem following his sarcastic celebration of Bitcoin's recent performance.

In a recent post shared earlier today, Schiff congratulated Bitcoin supporters as Bitcoin's biggest buyer, Strategy, marks six years of steady Bitcoin buying.

Known for his constant criticism of Bitcoin and its major holders, especially Michael Saylor, Schiff has mocked Strategy following Bitcoin's severe price crash recently.

He stressed that the firm's six-year Bitcoin accumulation has yet to deliver the returns Saylor previously promised.

Schiff further highlighted Strategy's profit margin, noting that the big Bitcoin buyer is now sitting close to its average purchase price at around $75,500 per BTC.

As such, the Bitcoin critic questioned the performance of Saylor's long-term investment strategy and its future potential, particularly given his previous expectations of annual returns of around 30%.

Following his assertions, Schiff further introduced his long-standing Bitcoin vs. Gold argument, suggesting that Saylor made the wrong choice of asset for his investments.

He argued that investing in gold instead would have doubled Saylor's money over the same period, triggering fresh debates across the crypto ecosystem.

Schiff's statement does not come as a surprise, as he is publicly known for repeatedly questioning Bitcoin's potential as a long-term investment.

The sworn Bitcoin critic has often argued that precious metals, especially gold, offer a more reliable store of value than Bitcoin, consistently describing Bitcoin as a Ponzi scheme.

#5202. Aave Buys Avon Team to Bring More Fixed-Rate Lending Onchain

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: Aave Labs said November 6 it had acquired Avon’s team and technology, including fixed- and floating-rate lending systems and an onchain order book. The move adds expertise for fixed-rate borrowing, but does not mean a finished fixed-rate Aave product has launched for every user.

Most people who take out a conventional loan want a clear answer to one question: what interest rate will they pay over the borrowing period? Decentralised finance often works differently, with interest rates that vary as supply and demand change within a lending pool. Aave Labs wants to broaden that model and has taken a new step towards it.

In its November 6 acquisition announcement, the company said it had bought the team and technology behind Avon, a developer focused on fixed-rate and floating-rate lending markets. The transaction includes Avon’s onchain order book, protocols, domains and other related assets. Financial terms were not disclosed.

The Aave release says Avon engineers Prince Sinha and Ayush Kaul will join the engineering team, while Omar el-Ashaal will join business development. Their stated focus includes fixed-rate lending and new types of onchain credit products.

A fixed rate can help borrowers plan expenses and hedge uncertainty. But supplying a dependable fixed-rate loan onchain requires more than setting a number at origination. Liquidity must be available over the loan’s term, interest-rate risk has to be allocated, collateral can change in value and lenders must be compensated for taking duration and credit-related risks.

Avon’s order-book technology could be relevant because an order book offers a different way to match borrowing and lending preferences compared with a purely pool-based floating-rate market. The acquisition does not itself prove that a design has completed security audits, won any required governance approval or become accessible to ordinary customers.

Aave describes itself as the largest onchain lending network, reporting more than $33 billion of deposits and $13 billion of active loans in its announcement. Those figures are company-reported and can fluctuate with token prices, collateral flows and market conditions.

The project already supports variable-rate borrowing and multiple asset markets. NewsBTC recently covered Aave’s protective freeze on new tBTC borrowing and supply, a reminder that risk settings and asset-specific decisions remain important even on an automated lending platform.

Fixed-rate capability could be useful to fintech partners and institutions that want more predictable liability management. It could also introduce complexities around maturity dates, rates, early repayment and liquidation that need careful contract design.

Aave Labs builds products that interact with the broader Aave network, but governance and the status of each deployed market matter. The acquisition is a corporate development and an engineering investment, not a guarantee that a new borrowing market is live today or approved across jurisdictions.

In NewsBTC’s prior reporting on Aave’s first externally curated V4 markets, the responsibility for risk settings and the structure of individual lending markets emerged as central questions. Fixed-rate lending will face similar scrutiny.

What should users watch for next? Published product specifications, audited contracts, governance decisions, clear rate and repayment terms, and evidence of sustainable liquidity. Aave’s acquisition explains the direction of travel: bringing more familiar credit-market features onchain. Whether users get the predictability they expect will depend on how those features are actually designed and deployed.

#5201. Best Crypto-Friendly Banks: 5 Options for Crypto Investors and Businesses

Home » Cryptopedia » Best Crypto-Friendly Banks: 5 Options for Crypto Investors and Businesses

Which banks will support your crypto habits?

Finding a crypto-friendly bank in 2026 is not as simple as opening an account with a traditional financial institution. Some banks allow transfers to cryptocurrency exchanges like Coinbase, while others offer built-in crypto trading, digital asset custody, or specialized banking services for Web3 businesses.

The best option depends on where you live, whether you are an individual investor or a crypto business, and which digital asset services you need. Some providers also impose restrictions on specific exchanges, transaction limits, or crypto-related business activities.

In this guide, we compare crypto-friendly banks and fintech providers, examining their supported services, availability, fees, and limitations to help you find the right banking solution in 2026.

A crypto-friendly bank makes it easier for individuals and businesses to interact with the cryptocurrency market through traditional financial services. Depending on the provider, this may include allowing transfers to and from crypto exchanges, offering crypto trading or payment features, or providing banking services to eligible crypto companies.

Not all crypto-friendly providers offer the same features. Some let customers buy, sell, and hold digital assets directly, while others simply support transfers to selected platforms like Kraken or provide financial services to Web3 businesses. Some are licensed banks, while others are fintech companies that partner with banks to deliver their services.

Before exploring the best crypto-friendly banks, consider the main advantages and potential drawbacks.

If you’re looking for the best crypto-friendly bank to join in 2023, you’re in the right place! This list breaks down some of the top banks for cryptocurrency from a variety of fields, including traditional banking, business banking, and personal accounts.

Revolut remains one of the more accessible options for people who want to manage traditional money and cryptocurrency through a single financial app.

Revolut is a digital banking platform that combines traditional financial services with cryptocurrency features. In supported markets, customers can use its app to buy, sell, hold, and transfer cryptocurrencies, including Bitcoin and Ethereum. Available assets and features depend on the customer’s country and account eligibility.

Revolut also supports crypto withdrawals and deposits through selected external wallets and blockchain networks. However, transaction fees, supported assets, and withdrawal limits vary. Its cryptocurrency services are provided by separate legal entities from its banking services, and crypto holdings are not covered by standard bank deposit protection.

Wirex flips the script amongst the other ‘best crypto-friendly banks’ contenders. While other platforms began their story as traditional banks and slowly moved to cryptocurrency acceptance, Wirex set out to be the world’s leading crypto bank from day one.

Wirex is a global fintech platform combining traditional currencies, cryptocurrency services, and payment cards. Users in eligible markets can buy, sell, exchange, and hold supported digital assets, as well as use available payment services to spend their funds.

Wirex also hosts a cross-chain bridge, offers crypto-linked cards, and a Cryptoback rewards programme, which advertises rewards of up to 8% on eligible purchases.

Actual rewards depend on the card programme, subscription, and applicable conditions. Wirex is not a traditional bank; its services are provided through regulated entities and financial partners, depending on the customer’s location.

Mercury is a US-based financial technology company providing business banking services through partner banks. It is particularly relevant to eligible cryptocurrency and Web3 startups, DAOs, and investment funds that need traditional financial infrastructure to manage their operations.

Businesses can use Mercury to manage fiat deposits, payments, expenses, and transfers to supported cryptocurrency exchanges, including Coinbase and Gemini. However, Mercury does not hold cryptocurrencies or provide crypto trading services.

It supports most eligible US-registered businesses, including those founded by international entrepreneurs, but does not accept applications from crypto exchanges or money services businesses.

Monzo is a UK-based digital bank that allows customers to use their accounts with selected cryptocurrency exchanges.

Although it does not offer native cryptocurrency trading, customers can transfer money to supported platforms, including Coinbase, making it an option for investors who want to manage traditional banking and crypto purchases separately.

Monzo applies transaction monitoring and restrictions to cryptocurrency-related payments. For example, it currently blocks payments to Binance while allowing transfers to a range of other exchanges. Its cryptocurrency payment allowance is also limited, so customers should check the latest terms before making large transactions.

Ally Bank is a US-based digital bank offering traditional banking products, including checking accounts, savings accounts, and investment services. Unlike specialist crypto-friendly financial platforms, Ally does not provide direct cryptocurrency trading or cryptocurrency custody through its banking services.

Customers cannot transfer cryptocurrencies directly to Ally accounts, which accept transactions in US dollars. Ally Invest also does not currently support direct cryptocurrency trading, although customers can access certain crypto-related investment products, including cryptocurrency funds and publicly traded companies involved in the industry.

Ally is therefore better suited to customers looking for conventional banking and investment services rather than direct access to cryptocurrency exchanges or digital assets.

Choosing a crypto-friendly bank involves more than finding a financial institution that allows cryptocurrency transactions. The right option depends on your location, investment activities, and whether you need basic exchange transfers or more advanced crypto-related services.

Here are five important factors to consider:

Check whether the bank supports transfers to your preferred cryptocurrency exchanges, such as Coinbase, Kraken, or Binance. Some financial institutions restrict payments to specific platforms or apply additional security checks to crypto-related transactions.

Compare account maintenance fees, international transfer costs, currency conversion charges, and any limits on cryptocurrency-related payments. Some providers also charge separate fees for buying, selling, or withdrawing digital assets.

Look for providers with appropriate regulatory authorisation and transparent security policies. Check whether eligible fiat deposits are covered by a recognised deposit guarantee scheme and understand that cryptocurrency holdings generally receive different protections.

Crypto-related banking services are not available everywhere. Before opening an account, check whether the provider accepts customers in your country and whether its cryptocurrency features are accessible in your region.

If you operate a Web3 startup, blockchain company, or cryptocurrency-related business, check the provider’s business eligibility requirements. Some platforms, including Mercury, support eligible technology and Web3 companies but exclude certain activities, such as operating a cryptocurrency exchange or money services business.

Crypto-friendly banks and financial platforms make it easier to manage traditional money and cryptocurrency in one place. However, their safety depends on regulation, banking partnerships, and security measures.

An important distinction is that eligible fiat deposits may be protected by deposit guarantee schemes, while cryptocurrencies generally are not. Crypto assets also carry risks such as market volatility, custody issues, and potential losses.

Not every crypto-friendly platform is a licensed bank. Providers such as Wirex and Mercury may operate through regulated entities or partner banks. Before choosing one, check its licences, account terms, and what protections actually apply to your funds.

Several financial institutions and fintech platforms support cryptocurrency-related transactions. Revolut and Wirex offer crypto services in eligible markets, while Monzo supports transfers to selected cryptocurrency exchanges. Mercury provides banking services to eligible Web3 businesses, whereas Ally Bank primarily offers traditional banking and investment products.

The best option depends on your needs. Revolut is suitable for individuals seeking integrated crypto services, Wirex focuses on cryptocurrency payments and spending, and Mercury caters to eligible Web3 businesses. Monzo can be useful for UK customers transferring money to supported exchanges.

Yes, many banks and financial platforms allow transfers to Coinbase, although availability depends on the customer’s location, payment method, and account restrictions. Always check your bank’s current cryptocurrency payment policy and Coinbase’s supported funding methods before initiating a transfer.

Some institutions also block transactions involving specific exchanges. Customers should review their provider’s terms and conditions before making crypto-related transfers.

However, cryptocurrency holdings are generally not covered by traditional bank deposit insurance. Fintech platforms may also use partner banks, so customers should identify the institution actually holding their deposits and verify the applicable protection.

"Red Alarm" aims to improve user protection by highlighting potentially high-risk dApps and contracts, providing users with more information to consider before interacting with them.

It reserves a share of each block's gas capacity for eligible payments, protecting that capacity from general traffic. The default reservation is 5% of each block's gas limit.

Payment Lane sets aside capacity for qualifying BNB transfers between wallets, so unrelated activity cannot use it all. Token payments can also qualify once approved for the lane.

Hyperliquid [HYPE] fell below the $80-mark, a week after reclaiming the level as support. In fact, institutions are continuing to sell the altcoin too. Even though the team has been countering the selling pressure through token burns.

#5198. Why Bitcoin's Next Soft Fork Could Feel Like a Practice Run

Why Bitcoin's Next Soft Fork Could Feel Like a Practice Run

Four flaws in Bitcoin’s consensus rules that have lingered for years.

Issues like quantum resistance may command attention, but a practical cleanup is what many experts think Bitcoin’s next soft fork is likely to address, spanning fixes for vulnerabilities in the network’s difficulty adjustment mechanism to denial-of-service vectors among miners.

Proposed in 2019 and known as the Great Consensus Cleanup (BIP-54), the upgrade seeks to address the “cruft” in Bitcoin’s consensus design through changes that have historically been sidelined, as described by long-time Bitcoin developer and BIP-54 co-author Matt Corallo.

“There’s a laundry list of things we know of that we really need to clean up,” he said during a recent appearance on MARA Foundation TV. “All of these issues are real concerns that could impact Bitcoin negatively, but they’re not like, ‘Oh, the sky is falling.’”

Although a formal timeline for the BIP’s undertaking hasn’t been established, Corallo thinks there’s an 80% chance that the Great Consensus Cleanup is up next for the network. During his talk with Head of MARA Foundation Isabel Foxen Duke, he also called it a practical exercise for Bitcoin developers who haven’t yet been through a soft fork.

At its core, Corallo said BIP-54 “fixes some stuff that everyone agrees are problems,” while targeting four specific vulnerabilities:

Still, not everyone views a maintenance-focused soft fork as a major win. On X, Paul Sztorc, co-founder and CEO of LayerTwo Labs, called BIP-54’s potential activation “kind of depressing, since it is a janitorial maintenance fork that doesn't do anything.”

Surveying developers at TABconf in Atlanta, Sztorc noted that Bitcoin’s “best and brightest” predominantly expect the Great Consensus Cleanup to be Bitcoin’s next soft fork anyway, with the earliest estimate in September 2027, and most pointing to 2028 or later.

Following the activation of Taproot in 2021, Bitcoin’s community has faced years of gridlock surrounding protocol changes. This friction is frequently framed around “ossification,” with broader protocol changes like OP_CAT (BIP-347) and OP_CHECKTEMPLATEVERIFY (BIP-119) struggling to reach ecosystem-wide consensus.

According to Corallo, soft forks have always involved overcoming technical hurdles. But more recently, he’s observed political and social friction that’s become just as relevant.

The Bitcoin community spent months debating how Taproot should be activated, with arguments pitting miner-signaling thresholds against mandatory user flag days. Corallo argues that BIP-54 can establish a deployment playbook for future soft forks.

“In every recent soft fork, there’s also been a bunch of drama about activation mechanisms,” he said. “So hopefully we could do a soft fork to go through that drama, and then copy and paste and reuse it and head off some of that drama [for future soft forks].”

In that sense, BIP-54 is just as much about proving Bitcoin can still agree on upgrades at the social layer as it is about actual code cleanup.

André Beganski is a Senior Content Manager at MARA Foundation, producing content on topics including the intersection of quantum computing and cryptography. He has previously worked for Decrypt.

#5197. Ripple Joins BlackRock, Standard Chartered in New Tokenization Report

Ripple contributed to a white paper commissioned by the Dubai Financial Services Authority (DFSA), which brings together perspectives and  insights from leading practitioners across the financial services ecosystem.

Ripple's Managing Director, Middle East & Africa, Reece Merrick, recently stated his contribution to  the DFSA's new publication: "Scaling Tokenization: The Road Ahead," alongside leaders from BlackRock, Standard Chartered and Robinhood.

The white paper considers the conditions that may support the responsible scaling of tokenised financial assets and related market infrastructure, including within the Dubai International Financial Centre (DIFC).

Aside from Merrick, other contributors include Tony Ashraf, Managing Director, Head of Technology & Operations and Head of Digital Assets, Middle East & Africa at BlackRock; Coy Garrison, Senior Director and Deputy General Counsel, Crypto at Robinhood; René Michau, Global Head, Digital Asset Advisory at Standard Chartered;  Henson Orser, Founder and Chief  Executive at Soter Insure; and Herman Schueller, Director, Innovation and Technology Risk Supervision at DFSA.

According to the DFSA report, tokenization of traditional financial assets has the potential to become a significant development in mainstream financial markets, offering the prospect of greater access for investors, increased efficiency for issuers and intermediaries, and reduced risk in certain areas, especially around settlement, for the wider financial system.

Merrick shared his perspective, saying that the real shift is not just tokenized treasuries and MMFs (Money Market Funds) gaining AUM but rather their on-chain utility as collateral in repo and margin without needing to redeem to cash first. Merrick anticipates massive growth in this regard over the next 12 months, predicting that a significant proportion of that will be on the XRP Ledger.

"With clear frameworks, institutional custody, and trusted settlement assets like RLUSD, the UAE, DIFC and Ripple are positioned at the forefront of this next phase," Merrick stated.

Forecasts for the future size of tokenised assets vary considerably. Citi Institute's base case is for the global market in tokenised financial assets to reach $5.5 trillion by 2030.  A Standard Chartered and Synpulse paper estimated that demand for tokenised assets could reach $30.1 trillion by 2034.

#5196. Argentine Bitcoiners Debut “The Genesis of Satoshi Nakamoto” Artwork in Downtown Buenos Aires

“The Genesis of Satoshi Nakamoto,” a 2.4-meter, multi-dimensional cube, was presented on October 29 at Polo Dot as part of the Open Fest of LABITCONF 2026. Driven by Bitcoin.ar through the Premio B·Arte, the work combines art, technology, and education in a journey through Bitcoin’s fundamental principles. The inauguration featured guided remarks by Rodolfo Andragnes and a musical performance by Mayumaná, according to an announcement by the organization.

Buenos Aires now has a monument dedicated to Satoshi Nakamoto, the person—or group of people—behind the creation of Bitcoin, whose identity remains unknown.

Driven by Bitcoin.ar through the fifth edition of the Premio B·Arte, the monument was conceived not only as a tribute to Satoshi, but also as an artistic and educational experience. Through its five faces—four lateral and one upper—it proposes a journey to approach different concepts that explain Bitcoin’s origin, functioning, and philosophy.

During the inauguration, Rodolfo Andragnes — co-founder of Bitcoin.ar and LABITCONF — accompanied the public with words and explanations about the details of the work, guiding the reading of its various elements and the meaning behind each representation. The presentation was accompanied by a musical show by Mayumaná, which added rhythm and sound to the opening of this new space dedicated to Bitcoin’s history.

Each of the monument’s faces offers a gateway to a different aspect of Bitcoin. The figure of Satoshi Nakamoto, for example, represents the anonymous character of its creator and the collective dimension of a technology that, once set in motion, continued to grow thanks to thousands of people distributed around the world. The reflections and multiplied geometric forms also evoke the structure of a network that replicates and expands.

Another of its faces addresses Bitcoin’s programmed scarcity through a cube that successively divides into halves in a Fibonacci sequence. The representation refers to “the halving”, the mechanism that approximately every four years halves the reward miners receive for validating blocks and that regulates the issuance of new bitcoins. Starting at 50 bitcoins per block, the total reward gets cut in half every four years for roughly 130 years, until 21 million bitcoins have been emitted and the block reward becomes zero. This supply limit is iconic to the cryptocurrency and key to its qualities as hard money.

The work also incorporates the first words of Bitcoin’s foundational document, the white paper published by Satoshi Nakamoto in October 31st,  2008. There, the possibility of making electronic payments directly between people, without needing to go through a financial institution, is proposed. That idea constitutes a foundational principle of the technology.

Another face reproduces information from the genesis block, the first block of the Bitcoin chain, created on January 3, 2009. Among its elements appears the reference to the headline from The Times newspaper that Satoshi incorporated into that block, a historical mark that places Bitcoin’s birth in its context and anchors it to history and time.

Thus, the monument proposes something more than contemplating a sculpture: it invites one to walk around it, observe it from different angles, and discover the concepts behind each of its parts; “The intention is to bring Bitcoin closer to those who are already part of its community, but also to awaken the curiosity of those who do not yet know its history.”

The monument was created by Ricardo Alonso, engineer, and Celeste Difabio Videla, architect, a couple of artists from Bariloche who won the fifth edition of the Premio B·Arte. The work measures 2.40 meters in width, length, and height, and is made of stainless steel and glass.

Its construction involved teams and collaborators from different localities across the country, in a process that combined different trades, materials, and techniques. Aída Pippo, visual artist, researcher of new technologies applied to art, and member of the Board of Directors of Bitcoin.ar, led the project and coordinated the different stages of production and assembly.

“It gives me great pride to feel that this is something we can leave in our city and in our country,” Andragnes expressed during the presentation, highlighting the value of a collective initiative that seeks to leave a cultural and educational footprint.

For their part, the creators noted that they sought for the work to function as a meeting point for the community and, at the same time, as a gateway for those who do not yet know Bitcoin. “You don’t always have the possibility of leaving something for posterity,” they shared when speaking of the experience of seeing the monument finished and installed in Buenos Aires.

With this inauguration, Bitcoin.ar seeks for the monument to become a permanent resource for the city: a space that schools, universities, families, and visitors can approach to learn Bitcoin’s history, discover its principles, and open new conversations about the future of money and technology. The intention is that what is presented today as an artistic work can continue to grow through educational activities and new experiences, and remain in Buenos Aires as a legacy for future generations.

The project was financed by the Fundación Bitcoin Iberoamérica and represents a new way of bringing Bitcoin’s history and principles to public and cultural spaces, beyond screens and specialized environments.

#5195. Bitcoin Price Prediction: Can BTC Reclaim $82,000 as ETF Inflows Slip to $56 Billion?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Can Bitcoin climb back to $82,000? A recent forecast says BTC must reclaim that level before any real recovery, even as cumulative spot Bitcoin ETF inflows slip to about $56 billion.

That makes the next few weeks pivotal for any Bitcoin price prediction.

BTC trades in a narrow range well below its record high, and a possible Fed rate cut is the catalyst traders now watch.

Traders who position for that move also keep an eye on early-stage projects.

One of them is MEMASA ($MEMASA), an upcoming AI trading app that lets users research and trade crypto through a chat.

Bitcoin has drifted lower for a month, and a recent 24/7 Wall St analysis says the key test now sits near $82,000.BTC needs a sustained close above that level, the analysis argues, before any real recovery can take hold.

The main reason is weak fund flows, as cumulative spot Bitcoin ETF inflows have slipped to about $56 billion. Only a couple of weeks earlier, that total was higher, which means large investors have been taking money out rather than adding.

Monetary policy offers little help either, since the Fed’s benchmark rate recently moved up instead of down. Still, the analysis sees a return above $100,000 as plausible within the next couple of years, if ETF demand and Fed cuts line up.

Bitcoin now trades near $76,000, down almost 9% over the past month after a steady slide.

Nearest strong support sits near $73,500, and technical indicators lean bearish, so a retest of that floor looks likely.

For any near-term Bitcoin price prediction, losing that floor could open a path toward $70,000.

A move above resistance near $84,400, backed by returning ETF inflows, would change the picture for bulls.

MEMASA is an upcoming AI crypto trading app that lets users analyze markets, check token risks, and trade on-chain by chatting with an AI assistant.

The app tackles problems familiar to crypto traders: too many charts, scattered market data, scam tokens, and complicated interfaces. MEMASA plans to combine market intelligence, risk checks, automated strategies, and trading in one self-custodial app.

If the crypto market enters another bullish phase, growing interest in altcoins could bring more people into trading. MEMASA aims to serve that audience by making market research and trading as simple as a conversation.

Its potential appeal extends from beginners struggling with trading interfaces to experienced traders who want faster access to market insights.

$MEMASA is the utility token powering the MEMASA ecosystem. Its planned uses include premium AI intelligence, trading fee discounts, marketplace payments, and staking for analysts and AI agents.

The token’s potential derives from stronger sentiment across the crypto market and growing demand within the MEMASA ecosystem. If the app attracts a wide audience, it might drive the demand for $MEMASA.

MEMASA is currently offering early access to $MEMASA through an on-chain early sale, with the present stage set at $0.0155, below the project’s anticipated public listing price. Earlier stages offer lower prices, while later stages provide faster token unlocks.

A Bitcoin rally could create favorable conditions for MEMASA’s debut. The bigger test will be whether the app delivers on its promise to simplify crypto trading and attracts enough users to build lasting demand for its token.

Bitcoin’s next move hinges on whether buyers defend support near $73,500. A push back above $82,000, with ETF inflows returning, would give bulls their first real win.

BTC has still more than doubled over three years, so this slump follows a long climb. A Fed rate cut remains the catalyst to watch.

MEMASA faces a different test, as it needs to turn an AI trading concept into an app people use regularly. If it succeeds, demand for its utility token could grow with the ecosystem. Both Bitcoin’s path and MEMASA’s outcome remain uncertain, and higher potential comes with higher risk.

#5194. Arkansas Sues Kalshi, Joining a Growing List of States Targeting Prediction Markets

Arkansas Sues Kalshi, Joining a Growing List of States Targeting Prediction Markets

Arkansas has sued Kalshi, accusing the prediction market of offering an illegal, unregulated gambling platform to the state's residents, Attorney General Tim Griffin said on Wednesday.

The lawsuit, filed against KalshiEX LLC and Kalshi Inc., alleges multiple violations of the Arkansas Deceptive Trade Practices Act. The state is seeking restitution, penalties, taxes and an injunction.

Today I filed a lawsuit against @Kalshi for violating the Arkansas Deceptive Trade Practices Act by operating an unregulated gambling platform.

Kalshi has built a global business that it calls a prediction market, allowing consumers to wager money on the outcome of just about… pic.twitter.com/ePycNKXzy6

— Attorney General Tim Griffin (@AGTimGriffin) November 4, 2026

"It doesn't take a genius to understand that wagering money on whether the Razorbacks win or lose is gambling," Griffin said. "And gambling on an unregulated platform is squarely prohibited by Arkansas law."

Instead of obtaining licenses and paying the taxes required of gambling operators, Kalshi opened its platform to Arkansas residents as young as 18 and encouraged them to bet on sporting and entertainment events, Griffin alleged. "Arkansas law is concerned with substance, not semantics," he said.

Griffin pointed to Kalshi's own responsible-gambling tools, including self-exclusion, deposit limits and trading breaks, along with its partnership with the National Council on Problem Gambling, as evidence that the company knows its platform carries gambling-style risks. "Although Kalshi insists that its products are not gambling, its actions speak louder than its labels," he said.

Kalshi, which is regulated by the Commodity Futures Trading Commission, has argued in other cases that its event contracts fall under exclusive federal jurisdiction and are not subject to state gambling laws.

Arkansas is the latest of more than a dozen states to take on prediction markets. Among the most significant cases:

Other states have also moved against prediction markets. Arizona filed criminal charges against Kalshi in March, a Washington court temporarily blocked its sports contracts in July, and Massachusetts' case is now before the state's highest court after a lower court barred Kalshi's sports contracts in January.

The CFTC has sued at least 10 states to defend what it says is its exclusive authority over prediction markets, and President Donald Trump has backed the agency, calling several state officials who challenged the platforms "scum."

Federal appeals courts are split. The Third Circuit ruled in April that New Jersey cannot regulate Kalshi's sports contracts, while the Sixth and Ninth Circuits sided with Ohio, Tennessee and Nevada. New Jersey has asked the Supreme Court to settle the question, backed by 39 other states and the District of Columbia, as well as the NFL. Kalshi's response to the petition is due November 9, and the Supreme Court is not expected to decide whether to take up the case until December at the earliest, ESPN reported.

#5193. Dogecoin Contributor Flags Social Signing as Potential Wallet Security Risk

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Dogecoin community member Mishaboar has raised concerns about the security risks associated with social signing, a feature that allows users to create and access a blockchain wallet using everyday accounts like Google, Apple ID, Telegram, or email instead of managing a complex seed phrase.

This comes after Keplr, a multichain wallet supporting Cosmos, Bitcoin, Ethereum, and Starknet, stated it had received reports of unauthorized transactions on some Keplr accounts created with Google or Apple login.

The wallet provider stated that the incident has been escalated to the Web3Auth team, with both working together to investigate the matter.

Before Keplr's disclosure, Web3 wallet infrastructure provider Web3Auth said it was aware of a security issue affecting Web3Auth's services used by some third parties. It stated that it had temporarily paused the embedded wallet service in response to the issue; however, after an initial investigation, no evidence was found that MetaMask users were impacted.

In his X post, Mishaboar criticized social signing. "Social signing, the feature you may want to add to your wallet if you hate your users," Mishaboar wrote.

Social signing, the feature you may want to add to your wallet if you hate your users. The worst part is that the users that use the social signing option -and which will end up being hacked through it - are also often newbies, entering crypto for the first time. And the risk goes beyond the funds in your wallet - data leaks could link your real life identity to your holdings, which can have terrible consequences.

The concern is particularly significant for inexperienced crypto users who may not recognize the risks associated with connecting their personal accounts to wallet services. While the feature makes wallet creation and access easier, it could introduce security vulnerabilities that users may not fully understand, Mishaboar argued.

"The worst part is that the users that use the social signing option—and who will end up being hacked through it—are also often newbies, entering crypto for the first time. And the risk goes beyond the funds in your wallet—data leaks could link your real-life identity to your holdings, which can have terrible consequences," Mishaboar stated.

Mishaboar noted that this warning also extends to wallets using social sign-in, which allows users to back up their keys in the "Cloud," which just means somebody else's server, he explained.

This week, a security advisory was issued for iOS users, who are urged to update their devices to enhance their wallet security. This follows security researchers identifying two exploit kits, Coruna and DarkSword, which target devices running older iOS versions. These do not rely on a flaw in any wallet app, but once a device is compromised, they can access wallet data stored on it.

#5191. South Korean Crypto Exchange Gopax Suspends Withdrawals and Trading After Hacking Threat

South Korea's Gopax halted standard withdrawals at 2:55 p.m. and trading at 3:53 p.m. Friday after a hacking-threat email, and is transferring all held assets to cold wallets with no loss reported so far.

South Korean cryptocurrency exchange Gopax suspended withdrawals and trading on Friday after receiving a hacking-threat email, and said it is moving all held virtual assets into offline cold wallets as a preemptive step to protect customer funds.

Gopax halted standard deposit and withdrawal services at 2:55 p.m. local time and stopped trading at 3:53 p.m., Yonhap News Agency reported, citing the exchange. Direct withdrawals through the website and app are unavailable. Customers who want coins out must apply through the customer support center, where requests are processed manually after internal verification.

“To ensure the safe custody of user assets, we plan to transfer all virtual assets to cold wallets for storage,” Gopax said in remarks carried by Seoul Economic Daily. “Direct withdrawals through the Gopax website or app are temporarily unavailable, and customers wishing to withdraw funds should apply through the Gopax customer support center.”

No actual loss of assets has been reported. The company has not set a date to reopen trading or standard withdrawals.

With trading offline, all open orders were canceled, Chosun Biz reported. Deposits were initially paused with withdrawals; later Korean coverage said deposits had been restored while web and app withdrawals stayed shut. Manual withdrawals through support will take longer than usual.

A cold wallet is a storage setup kept offline, cut off from the internet that hot wallets use to process customer sends. Gopax said the shift of remaining hot-wallet balances into cold storage is meant to shrink the surface available to anyone who can reach online systems.

A Gopax official told Yonhap the email came from a party claiming it could hack the exchange, but that the threat itself had not been verified. “We ask for your understanding, as this measure is intended to prevent financial incidents and protect user assets,” the company said.

The freeze lands in a week when exchange security is already under a bright light. On Oct. 29, attackers drained about $6.34 billion from Binance hot wallets in what Binance later described as a months-long campaign that began with a fake job interview. The FBI has tied that theft to North Korea; Binance has reopened withdrawals in stages and said it is covering customer losses from its own funds.

Brazil’s Mercado Bitcoin froze deposits and withdrawals earlier this week after its own security incident, with preliminary estimates of suspicious transfers near $40 million. Gopax’s case is different on the facts disclosed so far: the exchange is describing a threat email and a precautionary lockdown, not a confirmed drain.

Gopax itself has been dealing with account trouble. From early September, abnormal access hit some user accounts, leaking personal information on seven customers and two current or former employees, with further leakage confirmed last month, Yonhap’s Korean dispatch said. The company has treated that breach as separate from Friday’s threat email; any link has not been confirmed.

South Korea’s Financial Services Commission has also been pressing domestic platforms after a broader wave of cybersecurity incidents across financial firms. Korean exchanges are already required to keep at least 80% of customer crypto in cold storage. Friday’s move pushes Gopax further offline while it checks whether the threat is real.

For bitcoin holders on Gopax, the practical effect is a queue: coins are being parked offline while support staff approve outbound sends one by one. That is slower than an app button, and it is the trade the exchange says it is making to keep balances intact.

The test for Gopax now is whether it can finish the cold-wallet transfer, clear the manual withdrawal queue without a loss, and say when trading comes back — or whether the unverified threat turns into the incident it was trying to get ahead of.

#5187. Bitcoin Price Holds Near $75,000 After Hot October Jobs Report

U.S. employers added 165,000 jobs in October, nearly three times the 59,000 economists expected, the Labor Department said Friday, and the bitcoin price briefly pushed above $76,100 before settling near $75,500, Bitcoin Magazine Pro data shows.

The unemployment rate edged down to 4.1% from 4.2%, beating the Dow Jones forecast that called for it to hold. Average hourly earnings rose 0.4% on the month and 3.1% over the year, both ahead of estimates, CNBC reported.

Prior months were revised higher as well. August payrolls moved to 186,000, an upward revision of 53,000, and September rose to 60,000 after a 31,000 upgrade, leaving the two-month total 84,000 stronger than first reported.

The print lands on a market still digesting Thursday’s Nasdaq-led rout. The bitcoin price had held near $76,000 into that close, dipped toward $75,200 late in the session, then recovered into the mid-$75,000s overnight before the 8:30 a.m. ET release. Spot bitcoin changed hands around $75,800 after the data, with a market cap of about $1.52 trillion and roughly $48 billion in trading volume over the past day, Yahoo Finance data indicated.

Bitcoin price’s first move was a quick spike above $76,100, then a slide toward $75,600 before it settled back near $75,800. The range on the day has run from about $75,240 to $76,280, leaving the market little changed from its pre-release level even as rate futures repriced.

That muted finish fits the pattern after big macro prints. Most of the damage to the bitcoin price this week came from equity risk-off, not from the labor market itself, and a hot payrolls number mainly reinforces the path the Fed was already pricing.

CME FedWatch odds put roughly a 79% chance of a 25-basis-point hike at the Dec. 9 meeting after the release, up from about 70% into the open. The next employment report is due Dec. 4, five days before that decision, and the signal points the same way the last Fed hold did: stronger hiring and firmer wages leave less room for another pause.

Thursday’s session still sets the floor under the bitcoin price. The Nasdaq Composite fell about 5.5% and dropped into correction territory, more than 10% below its recent high, while the S&P 500 lost about 4.4% and the Dow shed roughly 1,800 points, or 3.4%, CNBC reported.

The Magnificent Seven erased more than $1 trillion in market value. Nvidia alone lost more than 9%, wiping out roughly $440 billion in capitalization, as investors priced in a slower AI data-center buildout after the Democratic midterm sweep.

Bitcoin has traded with that AI-heavy complex for most of the year, so a correction in the Nasdaq has pulled the bitcoin price lower even when on-chain flows looked steady. Miners that leased power and floor space to AI tenants are caught in both trades.

Fund flows followed the risk-off tape. U.S. spot bitcoin ETFs posted $430.2 million in net outflows on Thursday, led by about $200 million from Fidelity’s FBTC and roughly $105 million from BlackRock’s IBIT, bringing week-to-date redemptions through Thursday to about $870 million.

Leverage kept the bitcoin price soft into the overnight. More than $1 billion in crypto positions were liquidated over 24 hours, mostly long bets built during Wednesday’s short-squeeze bounce that lifted bitcoin near $80,000.

Professional and business services led October hiring with 70,000 jobs, health care added 42,000, and transportation and warehousing gained 25,000. Manufacturing rose 19,000. The breadth undercuts the recent pattern in which health care carried most of the gains.

Policy risk has not eased either. House Democrats are already drafting their own market structure bill ahead of taking the committees in January, a backdrop that has hung over crypto equities since the midterm results first hit the bitcoin price.

Traders are watching whether the bitcoin price can defend the mid-$75,000 zone that held on election night and several times since August. A clean break would leave September’s low back in sight after a week that erased most of the post-election squeeze, now with a hotter labor market and firmer hike odds stacked on top.

At the time of writing, the bitcoin price is near $75,500.

#5186. Canadian Man Extradited to US Over Plot to Hijack University Supercomputers for Crypto

Canadian Man Extradited to US Over Plot to Hijack University Supercomputers for Crypto

A Canadian man has been extradited to the United States to face charges that he helped hack into university computers to steal login credentials and secretly mine cryptocurrency, the Justice Department said on Tuesday.

A federal grand jury in the Western District of New York indicted Ryan James Roach, 35, of Saskatchewan, and Mathiew James Stubbings, 41, of Ontario, on charges of conspiracy to obtain information from and damage a protected computer, obtaining information from a protected computer, and intentionally damaging a protected computer. The charges carry a maximum sentence of 10 years in prison and a $250,000 fine.

Canadian man extradited to the U.S. for his role in computer hacking scheme targeting universities... @FBIBuffalo @USDOJ_Intl https://t.co/vY8IacXdZZ pic.twitter.com/NsSZ5BIkIf

Between June 2016 and December 2019, the pair targeted U.S. educational institutions with supercomputers, researching security weaknesses in their networks before breaking in, according to the indictment. Once inside, they allegedly deployed malware, stole login credentials and forced the victims' computers to mine cryptocurrency for their own benefit.

Victims included institutions in New York, Tennessee, North Carolina and Massachusetts, among them a university in the Western District of New York, prosecutors said. The pair allegedly used virtual private servers in several countries, registered under false names, to send malware and mining software to victim computers and store stolen credentials, and routed attacks through computers compromised at other victims to hide their identities.

Roach was extradited from Canada on October 28 and arraigned in Rochester, New York, where he was held pending a detention hearing. Extradition proceedings against Stubbings are continuing in Canada.

The scheme is a form of cryptojacking, in which attackers use other people's computers to mine crypto while the victims bear the electricity costs and slower performance. The Justice Department did not say which cryptocurrency the pair allegedly mined.

But hackers looking to profit from stolen computing power may be turning elsewhere. In a report published Monday, Google's Threat Intelligence Group said it expects attackers to "increasingly prioritize LLMJacking over traditional resource theft like cryptojacking," hijacking companies' access to AI models and passing on the bills.

Unlike the gradual drain of cryptojacking, LLMjacking can run up huge cloud charges within hours, Google said. In incidents investigated by its Mandiant unit this year, fraudulent charges topped $100,000 in a single day, and some organizations racked up $1 million before access was cut off. Attackers are reselling stolen access through underground services that Google calls "LLM Mixers."

Latest data from Token Terminal shows that Ethena has added a massive $337.4 million to its total market cap within just 24 hours, positioning it as a leader in the stablecoin market cap growth.

While this marks a notable shift from the ranking seen the previous day, when Ripple led the sector with a substantial increase of $34.7 million, Ripple has now slipped to the second position, ranking just behind Ethena in the market cap growth record today.

Per the data, Ripple has ceded the stablecoin growth dominance to Ethena as it has recorded a $39.6 million increase over the same 24-hour period.

Ondo also followed, recording a $4.2 million increase, followed by OpenStandard at $3.2 million and UnitedStables at $3 million.
