#5102. Bitcoin ETFs flip negative for 2026 after $1.28 billion outflow week, worst since June

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

The 12 U.S.-listed spot bitcoin funds have now lost $914.1 million for the year, while ether, Solana and Hyperliquid ETFs all extended weekly outflow streaks in a brutal turn for crypto ETFs.

U.S. spot bitcoin ETFs logged $1.28 billion in net outflows last week, their worst week since late June, per The Block's analysis of SoSoValue data. It was the funds' second straight negative week and made the top ten largest weekly outflows since they launched in January 2024 at #7.

The funds saw outflows every day except Wednesday last week, per the data. They lost $398.6 million on Monday, $115.6 million on Tuesday, $430.2 million on Thursday and $411.9 million on Friday, with Wednesday's relatively modest $74.6 million inflow marking the only positive day.

The most recent worse week ended June 26, when the funds shed $1.79 billion in what The Block reported at the time was their second-worst week on record. Bitcoin ETFs lost $202.6 million the week before last, including a $643.3 million Friday outflow that was the largest single-day exit in four months after the record Binance hack.

BlackRock's IBIT accounted for $403 million of Friday's loss, or about 88% of the day's gross outflows. Bitwise's BITB and Ark & 21Shares' ARKB each lost about $20 million, and Grayscale's GBTC shed $11.1 million. Fidelity's FBTC took in $42.8 million on Friday, after leading Thursday's outflows with $199.9 million, as The Block reported.

The week's outflows erased bitcoin ETFs' gains for the year. The funds entered the week with about $368 million in 2026 net inflows, after turning positive for the year in late September. Monday's outflows pushed that figure below zero, and the funds closed Friday at about $914.1 million in net outflows year to date.

The total value of bitcoin held across the 12 funds dropped 2.3% to $95.9 billion, equal to about 6.3% of the cryptocurrency's market capitalization. IBIT held $60.3 billion in net assets against $65.1 billion in cumulative inflows.

Bitcoin fell 5.6% on Thursday alone, and analysts linked the slide to the Democratic sweep of Congress and doubts over AI spending, The Block reported Friday. Analysts say Texas's blue wave in particular could result in slowed development in the state replete with bitcoin mining facilities and data centers.

Spot ether ETFs lost $365.3 million last week, per SoSoValue data, their sixth straight week of net outflows. The run has drained about $2.02 billion from the funds since late September. Ether ETFs have twice posted eight-week losing streaks, most recently when they matched that record in early July.

Like the bitcoin funds, ether ETFs had only one positive trading day last week, also on Wednesday, when they gained $53 million. Monday's $139.8 million outflow was the week's largest, followed by $100 million on Thursday and $93.2 million on Friday. BlackRock's ETHA accounted for $90.8 million of Friday's total, about 96% of the gross losses, mirroring the firm's bitcoin fund.

The products are now down about $405.6 million for 2026. Net assets fell 2.9% over the week to $12.84 billion, against $11.92 billion in cumulative net inflows.

Spot Solana ETFs lost $26.3 million last week, per SoSoValue, nearly all of it on Friday. It was their fifth straight negative week, the longest streak since the funds launched in October 2025, and brought five-week outflows to $165.4 million. The streak began the week the funds ended a record 14-week inflow run.

Bitwise's BSOL led Friday's Solana outflows with $14.4 million, followed by Grayscale's GSOL at $5.4 million. The funds held $1.31 billion in net assets as of Friday, down from $1.40 billion a week earlier, though they remain about $676.2 million positive for the year.

The three publicly traded Hyperliquid ETFs shed $15.6 million, also extending their outflow streak to five weeks, the longest since they began trading in May. The funds have lost $58.4 million over that stretch, and their net assets fell about 17% on the week to $374.1 million. Friday's $8 million outflow was led by Bitwise's BHYP.

Bitcoin traded near $75,880 on Saturday morning, while ether changed hands around $2,244, SOL near $89.84 and HYPE around $77.79, according to The Block's price pages.

#5097. Ex-FBI agent pleads guilty to stealing $2.47 million in crypto from wallets tied to 35 investigations

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Court filings show Patrick Yaroch drained at least 79 wallets, including some belonging to crime victims, and planned to use the money to buy a vineyard in Portugal.

Former FBI agent Patrick Steven Yaroch admitted on Thursday to stealing about $2.47 million in cryptocurrency from wallets tied to FBI investigations, according to the U.S. Attorney's Office for the Eastern District of Virginia.

Yaroch, 37, of Ashburn, Virginia, pleaded guilty to one count each of wire fraud and money laundering related to the thefts. Each count carries up to 20 years in prison, and sentencing is set for March 4, 2027.

Beginning no later than December 2024, Yaroch searched FBI databases, including a classified one, for crypto credentials, according to the statement of facts he signed. He used the information he found to make about 100 transfers out of at least 79 wallets connected to 35 FBI investigations.

Most of the wallets belonged to people under FBI investigation, and some were controlled by two adversarial nation states the filing does not name. NBC News reported in August, citing two sources, that the adversarial nation in the original complaint was Russia. Yaroch also made at least 10 transfers from wallets belonging to crime victims in other FBI cases, and at least one from a wallet the FBI had funded for an operation.

The filing calls Yaroch "indiscriminate," with individual thefts ranging from 81 cents to more than $580,000. After each theft, he kept checking FBI systems to make sure no one had noticed.

Yaroch joined the FBI's Boston Division in 2017, working on a squad focused on one of the two nation states. From February 2025 until July 31, 2026, he was a supervisory special agent in the Counterintelligence and Espionage Division at FBI headquarters.

Yaroch had racked up significant debts before the scheme began: he had taken out more than $100,000 in loans in 2024 and owed more than $45,000 on credit cards, according to the filing.

Between Nov. 25, 2025, and Jan. 13, 2026, Yaroch moved about $93,082 to a TD Bank account and used it to pay down debts, including about $30,176 on credit cards and $20,000 toward a Lexus, per the filing. Yaroch initially said he stole the crypto out of frustration, but prosecutors wrote in an August court filing that he "should not be viewed as a selfless Robin Hoodesque figure."

On June 4, Yaroch asked ChatGPT what it would do with about $1 million if it wanted to leave the U.S. and live in a European Union country. The chatbot's answer cited his wife and child, "the desire for a 2-5 hectare estate, interest in age-worthy red wine," and named Portugal "my top choice for your specific situation," according to the filing.

On June 15, Yaroch gave two Portuguese lawyers power of attorney to register him with the country's tax authority. He also arranged a September trip to Portugal tied to his planned vineyard purchase. Prosecutors also said in the August filing that Yaroch had failed to report a 10-day trip in May, when he flew to Germany and came home from Portugal.

The prosecutors asked to keep him jailed before trial, citing about $160,000 he still held on Kraken and possible Espionage Act charges that were never filed. U.S. District Judge Anthony Trenga instead released him on a $50,000 bond with location monitoring, under conditions that bar him from using video game consoles.

Yaroch initially agreed to give agents a piece of paper that appeared to contain his seed phrase, according to the August filing. However, he later asked for it back after agents told him he had the right to stay silent and that what he said could be used against him in a criminal case.

When agents found the wallet he had pointed them to, $1.02 million had already moved out. Yaroch then admitted the funds were on the lending platform Suilend and helped them reinstall a wallet app he had deleted from his phone.

Under the plea agreement, Yaroch must pay at least $2.4 million in restitution and faces a forfeiture judgment of at least $2.4 million. He also agreed to forfeit 17,990.67 USDC and about 1.38 million SUI seized on July 31, worth roughly $1.2 million at current prices, plus the contents of his TD Bank, Charles Schwab, Kraken and BloFin accounts.

Yaroch acknowledged that the full proceeds of the scheme "are not available." He also agreed to be interviewed by Justice Department and FBI officials about his crimes.

If there's eventually a Netflix documentary about the case, Yaroch won't be profiting: any money he makes from books, films, articles or interviews about his FBI work or his case will go to the U.S. government. He must also submit any such work to the FBI for review before publication.

The Block reported in August that Yaroch had been fired on July 31 and charged with transporting and receiving stolen money. At the time, the Justice Department's Office of the Inspector General said he had moved about $1 million in roughly 10 transfers and had reported himself to two Justice Department employees on July 29.

Earlier this year, the FBI arrested John Daghita in Saint Martin over the alleged theft of more than $46 million in crypto from the U.S. Marshals Service. Onchain investigator ZachXBT alleged that Daghita is the son of an executive at a firm contracted to help the agency manage seized crypto.

A federal grand jury in Alexandria indicted Daghita on 15 counts in March, and a French appeals court in Guadeloupe approved his extradition to the U.S. in May, according to local broadcaster RCI. His arraignment is set for Nov. 12 in Alexandria, according to his court docket.

#5096. Zcash Upgrade Could Lock $16 Million in Crypto Forever

Zcash Upgrade Could Lock $16 Million in Crypto Forever

At recent market prices, those holdings represent roughly $15 million to $16 million that could become effectively frozen when the network activates its NU7 upgrade.

“NU7 (block 3,543,405) makes v4 transactions invalid, so what remains in Sprout is unspendable: frozen, not burned,” Zecstats says in its information card for Sprout.

The upgrade is scheduled for block 3,543,405, expected Thursday evening in the United States. The exact deadline depends on how quickly the blockchain processes new blocks, rather than a predetermined clock time.

Unlike Bitcoin, where transactions are generally visible to anyone examining the blockchain, Zcash allows users to conceal transaction amounts and the addresses involved. It accomplishes this through technology called zero-knowledge proofs, which can verify that a transaction is valid without revealing its private details.

NU7 changes that by eliminating version-4 transactions, the only format capable of moving coins out of Sprout. According to the network's technical specification, transactions using that format will become invalid after activation.

The decision addresses a problem familiar to anyone maintaining old software: supporting obsolete technology creates additional complexity and potential security vulnerabilities.

Developers haven't technically destroyed the coins. They will continue counting toward Zcash's circulating issuance calculations, and a future network upgrade could theoretically restore access, but that is not guaranteed, and the team has not shown any interest in doing so.

In fact, the specification explicitly warns that spending those funds may never be re-enabled.

Legacy holders are the people most exposed to the change. Anyone whose ZEC remains in Sprout must move it through a compatible withdrawal mechanism before the upgrade activates, or risk losing access indefinitely.

Sprout addresses begin with "zc," which can help users recognize old wallet records. Regular ZEC holders using modern supported wallets do not need to migrate their coins out of Sprout, although keeping wallet software updated remains important.

The blockchain also cannot reveal who owns the remaining Sprout balance, meaning nobody knows how much belongs to active users and how much is already inaccessible because its owners lost their keys.

Meanwhile, NU7 brings a benefit for everyone else: Zcash's target block interval falls from 75 seconds to 25 seconds, meaning transactions should receive their first confirmation approximately three times faster, according to the Zcash Foundation.

For Sprout holders, however, the relevant number is block 3,543,405 which will happen later today. As of the time of publication, the latest available block is 3,543,027. Once the network reaches block 3,543,405, any remaining coins in the old pool will be frozen under the new rules.

After over five years at Binance, Kaiser Ng is leaving to "pursue a new venture." The firm's co-CEO said a transition plan had been in place for six months, which is long before the recent hack.

Binance Chief Financial Officer Kaiser Ng is stepping down and will be replaced by Justin Yek, a former finance executive at payments firm Airwallex, Binance co-CEO Yi He said in a statement on Saturday, Bloomberg reported.

#5090. How to Lose Fat and Build Muscle Sustainably as a Busy Mom—Your Realistic 4-Step Plan

A strength coach for moms outlines the key strength training and diet habits to adopt for lasting change.

For many moms returning to exercise after pregnancy, losing fat while building muscle can feel like a daunting goal.

“The odds are good that you lost some muscle mass during pregnancy. Maybe you weren’t training as much as you normally did or you weren’t training at all,” says Bailey, coach and founder of Well Mamas Coaching, in a recent YouTube video.

However, body recomposition is a gradual process, she explains, and consistency—“sticking with it”—matters far more than getting everything right.

“It's not [about] doing it perfectly. It's not having an hour to yourself every single day to do all this magical meal prep and green juicing,” she says.

“It's just jumping back in when you can and not buying into the start-stop cycle of...start[ing] over on Monday and find[ing] a new plan.”

Strength training with progressive overload is a “gold standard” pillar of a realistic body recomposition plan, Bailey says.

Progressive overload means gradually making your workouts more challenging over time by increasing the weight, number of repetitions, or training frequency, or by reducing your rest periods.

“This type of strength training is what signals your body...to adapt, change, and rebuild the muscle fibers that we're tearing down during these workouts, which is what helps you build muscle,” Bailey explains.

She recommends focusing on compound exercises such as squats, lunges, deadlifts, hip thrusts, and the bench press because “you're recruiting multiple muscle groups.”

As for your workout schedule, she reassures viewers that you don't need to train “six times a week...for 45 to 60 minutes.”

“Four workouts a week, 30 minutes or less, with progressive overload and compound moves...is plenty,” she says.

By now, it's well established that muscle growth and repair depend on eating enough protein.

“If you're not eating enough protein to repair the tears that you create in your muscles when you work out, then your body doesn't have the amino acids it needs to build those muscle fibers,” Bailey says.

She recommends aiming for around 30g of protein per meal. That's broadly in line with research showing that consuming around 25-30g of protein per meal is associated with greater lean mass and both upper- and lower-body strength in healthy women.

Good options include lean meat, beans, and legumes, yogurt, cottage cheese, and canned tuna.

“Mild” is the key word, Bailey emphasizes.

A calorie deficit means consuming fewer calories than your body burns each day.

“Of course, you do need to be in some sort of caloric deficit in order for fat loss to occur,” she reminds viewers.

However, the size of that deficit matters.

She recommends a moderate deficit of around 200-400 calories below your maintenance intake.

“That's just enough to prompt your body to use stored fat for energy,” she explains. “However, it's not so much that your body starts cannibalizing muscle.”

Research supports the importance of avoiding an overly aggressive calorie deficit, with one study finding that eating more than 500 fewer calories than required impaired gains in lean mass.

She adds one caveat for breastfeeding moms.

“You want to go above what I would calculate if you weren't nursing—anywhere from 400-800 additional calories per day,” she says.

Bailey acknowledges this can be difficult for moms.

“Often sleep and stress feel like they're not within our control,” she says.

However, sleep is essential because it's during recovery that muscle adapts and grows.

“The muscle...broken down through resistance training...is rebuilt...during sleep, while we're recovering,” she explains.

She also references research showing that sleep duration influences body recomposition, with shorter sleep associated with less fat loss.

If “a solid eight hours” isn't realistic, she suggests finding even a few extra minutes by cutting back on doomscrolling or late-night TV.

She also notes that chronically elevated cortisol levels associated with stress may interfere with muscle-building processes, recommending outdoor walks, deep breathing, or even guided meditation in the shower to create small moments of calm.

Kate puts together fitness content that covers functional and strength training, cardio, workout challenges, interviews and news. She's often doing gym laundry or listening to music.

#5089. Matt Damon and David Letterman Pop into The Gilded Age Just for Funzies

HBO’s period drama got a very modern surprise from two very recognizable faces.

IF YOU’RE NOT familiar with HBO’s The Gilded Age, it’s a pretty easy show to explain. Set in New York City during the titular era—the rapidly changing 1880s—the show is an exploration of the rich-people trials and tribulations of a new money family (led by Carrie Coon and Morgan “Train Daddy” Spector) and their neighbors, an old money family (led by Christine Baranski).

One of the show’s most important aspects is its production design, with intricately built sets and elaborately planned costumes helping to transport its viewers back nearly 150 years. Immersion is the name of the game.

With a setup like that, The Gilded Age is just about the last show where you’d expect to see a couple of our most recognizable modern stars walk directly across the screen—but that’s just what Matt Damon and David Letterman did, less than 10 minutes into the show’s season 4 premiere.

Damon, the Oscar-winning movie star who earlier this year led Christopher Nolan’s The Odyssey and Netflix’s The Rip, and Letterman, the legendary talk show host, walked across the screen in full costume, saying nothing, in a blink-and-you’ll-miss-it moment.

As it turns out, according to a story published by Vulture, the whole thing was born from Letterman’s lifelong desire to make a surprise appearance in something as a background actor, something that began way back in 1977 when he saw a fellow comedian friend slyly appear in the background of a scene in Annie Hall.

In recent years, Letterman has apparently come close to shooting background scenes in Wes Anderson’s The Phoenician Scheme, the upcoming Michael B. Jordan-directed version of The Thomas Crown Affair, and Black Rabbit alongside Jason Bateman—but for one reason or another, none of them came to be.

Letterman filmed an upcoming episode of his current show, the Netflix interview series My Next Guest Needs No Introduction with David Letterman, with Damon, and discovered that Damon and Ben Affleck began their careers as background actors, and, for the sake of the bit, figured this would be the perfect opportunity to see his dream come to fruition. “We thought, Okay, this could be the perfect opportunity,” Next Guest showrunner Isabel Richardson explained to Vulture. “It’d be really fun for Dave to do this with Academy Award–winner Matt Damon, and it tied into his origin story really well.”

Damon, luckily, was into the idea too—and they made it happen. “It was a kind of hysterical moment when our production supervisor Leah Baker came up to me on set and was like, ‘Well, I have a kind of funny, interesting question: Would you like Dave Letterman and Matt Damon to be background on our set one day?’” Gilded Age showrunner Churchill said in the same Vulture story. “I just started laughing.”

Surprise appearances and cameos have long been a part of Damon’s playbook. Marvel fans certainly remember his brief appearances in movies like Deadpool 2 and Thor: Ragnarok, while fans of Damon’s frequent collaborator Steven Soderbergh’s movies won’t forget his surprise turns in movies like No Sudden Move and Che: Part Two. He even appeared in a very brief role in Ethan Coen’s Drive Away Dolls after working with Ethan and his brother Joel on the 2010 hit True Grit.

Perhaps Damon’s most memorable (and truly most random) cameo comes in the 2004 comedy Eurotrip, though. He appears early in the film as Donnie, the lead singer of an alt-rock band who sings a song about the affair he was apparently having with the main character’s girlfriend. “Scotty Doesn’t Know” is a pillar of both 2000s pop-punk and that era’s teen sex comedy movement, and Damon’s completely out-of-nowhere cameo is a huge reason why.

And, look, HBO seems to enjoy this kind of thing anyway. Just last month, we spotted the University of Nebraska’s head football coach Matt Rhule in the final episode of Lanterns. Does his appearance make sense in a show set in Nebraska? Sure. Did we ever expect to see Matt Rhule in an acclaimed, highly watched HBO drama set in a superhero world? No, we certainly did not.

It’s fun (and probably a bit trippy at first) to come to grips with seeing who you think you’re seeing. And if, somehow, you don’t know who either of those two are, you probably won’t notice at all and won’t think a thing. And isn’t that how all great cameos should go, really?

#5088. Mercado Bitcoin Said Its Hack Was Contained, But Blockchain Data Tells a Different Story

Mercado Bitcoin Said Its Hack Was Contained, But Blockchain Data Tells a Different Story

An attacker drained Mercado Bitcoin-linked deposit addresses nearly four hours after the Brazilian exchange said it had contained a security breach, new blockchain research shows.

Bitquery's analysis, updated Thursday, traces more than $48 million in stolen assets across 11 blockchains. Its findings challenge the exchange's claim that it stopped the attack quickly.

The Bitcoin exchange announced at 9:56 a.m. Brazil time on November 2 that it had quickly contained the incident. Bitquery says 36,042 Ethereum customer deposit addresses were emptied between 9:27 a.m. and 1:56 p.m. Brazil time.

Those addresses held roughly $266,000, a small fraction of the total theft. But the attacker could still move those funds after the exchange’s announcement.

A customer deposit address works much like a personal receiving account for cryptocurrency. The exchange later gathers those coins into a shared online wallet, called a hot wallet, to process everyday transactions. Moving funds from a deposit address requires its private key, the secret that authorizes transfers.

Bitquery says the withdrawals were authorized directly by the deposit addresses, suggesting the attacker had access to their private keys or to a system capable of signing transactions on their behalf.

“That needs a key for each address, or a way into the system that signs for them,” the researchers explained. They warned that exchanges typically generate deposit keys from a single master key, meaning the attacker may still be able to access other customer addresses.

The amount stolen is also disputed. Initial estimates put the amount above $40 million, while Mercado Bitcoin's updated FAQ now puts affected assets at about $36.3 million. Bitquery estimates that $48.6 million was stolen across 11 blockchains.

Mercado Bitcoin's public updates have not explained the gap between its containment claim and the later transfers. The company assured customers it would cover user losses with its own funds.

Bitquery warns customers against reusing old deposit addresses until the exchange confirms they are safe. Mercado Bitcoin's reopening plan begins November 9 with balance viewing only; deposits, withdrawals and trading have no restart date.

#5084. 7 Pendant Lights to Perfect Your Overhead Lighting Scheme (2026)

Sure, recessed lighting serves a functional purpose, but pendant lights are where things get interesting. A quick sweep through any home tour, and you’ll recognize the strategically placed Louis Poulsen above the kitchen island, an Isamu Noguchi in the entryway, and a Herman Miller saucer pendant hovering over the living room that will steal the show. Below are seven pendant light fixtures that set the mood instead of harshening the vibe. We rounded up the best pendant lights for various design personalities, including a vintage-inspired showpiece and a few variations of low-key ambient lighting. Among them are most of the different types of pendant lights you’re likely to come across, with picks for modern minimalists, Belle Époque enthusiasts, and everyone in between.

First developed in 1958, the PH 5 is a classic for a reason. Renowned Danish lighting manufacturer Louis Poulsen designed the iconic pendant with a three-shade system to equally distribute light. While the pendant works for that functional purpose, it also doubles as an unconventional art piece. Choose from a number of monochromatic and multitone colorways, including this steel blue.

Look familiar? That’s because this large pendant, inspired by Noguchi’s Akaris, hangs in many an AD home tour, including above the dining room table in this Japandi-inspired Bay Area home and in the bedroom of this South African–inspired craft home, for example. There are a few reasons why this style of paper lighting is so popular: It goes with everything; it’s affordable; and it adds a sense of levity to the room with its ethereal shape and its soft, warm glow. As lantern pendants go, this option from Design Within Reach is about as low-commitment as they come. It works anywhere you want ambient lighting rather than a spotlight.

Designer pendant lights can cost upwards of $500, but you can pay a fraction of that with finds from Article, a direct-to-consumer furniture store that sells high-quality wares at more palatable prices. That’s certainly the case with the Aino pendant, with matte colorways and a simple angular silhouette that illuminates whatever you’re chef-ing up in your Scandi-style kitchen. The metal cone shade and braided fabric cord make it an easy pick for task lighting over a counter, and if you’re working with a tighter kitchen space, there’s also a mini pendant version for $99 that ships with an LED bulb.

This three-tiered ceiling light from West Elm adds visual texture to blank space, making it a welcome addition to transitional areas in need of personality. A piece like this can also help to add structure to an open plan where the kitchen and dining rooms collide. This piece comes in 26-inch and 32-inch sizes (the larger is a proper large pendant light that can hold its own over a dining table), as well as in hardwired and plug-in options, making it one of the few statement hanging lights you can install without calling an electrician.

Any cold kitchen space dominated by stone and steel fixtures could use a touch of handmade whimsy. Hanging this handwoven rattan pendant over a kitchen island promises to warm up the whole room. Usually, two or three pendant lights hang over a kitchen island; this single shade provides a cozy, welcoming alternative, softening the light rather than spotlighting the countertop, so your mise en place won’t feel so interrogated.

This midcentury Herman Miller pendant, aptly named a saucer for its elliptical shape, is prized by commerce writer Julia Harrison for its retro, Mad Men–esque appeal. Despite the resemblance to our Hay pick above, Miller wasn’t riffing on Noguchi with this pendant: He wanted a Swedish silk-covered pendant he couldn’t justify buying, so he made his own using a sprayed plastic skin originally developed for mothballing Navy ships (whereas the orbital Hay version uses rice paper). The vertical ribbing gives it a shape that seems to be almost expanding as it hangs. Its white shade and chrome hardware make it easily adaptable to many (if not most) design styles.

This lighting fixture brings to mind 10-foot ceilings, Flemish tapestries, and an echoey marble entryway. If you’re lucky enough to have all of the above, this is exactly the sort of pendant your space needs. If not, luckily, the fixture carries that whole vibe on its own, with its cream glass bowl, brass detailing, and a sculpted metal tassel that adds a bit of movement. A vintage-inspired piece like this transforms a more humble hallway into something dignified and scenic.

#5083. DOJ Creates 'Super Intelligence' Task Force to Guard Against AI Abuse

DOJ Creates 'Super Intelligence' Task Force to Guard Against AI Abuse

The U.S. Department of Justice is setting up a task force dedicated to policing the misuse of advanced artificial intelligence, as Washington races to build enforcement muscle around a technology it increasingly frames in near-sci-fi terms.

Announced Thursday, the Super Intelligence Task Force, or DOJ-SITF, will be led by Associate Attorney General Stanley Woodward and Associate Deputy Attorney General Stephen Weiss.

Its stated mission is to make sure American leadership in “super intelligence” isn’t compromised by bad actors, whether foreign governments and their proxies, transnational criminal organizations, or companies and individuals using AI to commit fraud or other crimes.

The department invoked President Donald Trump’s framing that “America stands at the forefront of a new technological revolution in intelligence.”

“SI development will continue to be powered by American innovators,” Attorney General Todd Blanche said in a statement, adding that the task force would work alongside SI Force Chair Jay Clayton to keep critical AI technologies from being “manipulated by bad actors at home or abroad.”

Blanche said the DOJ would keep “identifying and prosecuting individuals whose use of SI violates the law, including by maliciously targeting American taxpayers, critical U.S. infrastructure, or other aspects of our national security.”

Notably, the effort isn’t purely about catching criminals. Weiss said the department also has “a responsibility” to “harden regular reporting on SI risk and response, and ensure companies exercise vigorous internal controls so that SI technology behaves as intended,” signaling that the DOJ wants AI developers themselves to tighten safeguards against models going off the rails.

The term “super intelligence” typically refers to a hypothetical AI that surpasses human capability across the board, and the government’s embrace of the phrase marks an escalation in how officials talk about the technology. Former SEC chair Jay Clayton has been tied to a broader Trump administration push around super intelligence, and the SEC recently added “super intelligence” to its 2027 examination priorities.

The task force joins a widening federal scramble to get ahead of AI risk. Trump signed an executive order setting up a fight with states over AI regulation. Earlier this week, the U.S. banking regulator OCC issued its first AI framework as banks battle rogue agents reaching into customer accounts.

The DOJ did not specify a timeline or detail how the task force would coordinate with other agencies, saying only that its work would “promote innovation and prevent the misuse” of the technology.

#5077. OpenAI Promises to Foot AI’s Power Bill After Voter Revolt. Who Enforces It?

OpenAI Promises to Foot AI’s Power Bill After Voter Revolt. Who Enforces It?

OpenAI promised Thursday that neighbors of its future AI campuses won't foot the bill for their electricity needs, two days after voters dealt data center expansion a blow at the polls. It also pledged public energy and water figures and limits on secrecy agreements with officials.

Its National Community Compact covers future campuses (a fancy rebranding for the term AI data centers) OpenAI both owns and operates, according to the company’s announcement. A separate policy blueprint asks legislators and regulators to apply similar requirements across the industry.

“We will pay the full cost of powering our compute campuses and ensure local residents do not foot the bill for our energy needs,” OpenAI said in its “national community compact.”

It also promises financial assurances before costly grid upgrades, so other customers aren't left paying if projects shrink or fail. Each AI data center would get a water plan, annual progress reports and independent noise studies.

The national compact does not describe penalties for noncompliance, a dispute process or independent verification of electricity and water figures. Residents could read OpenAI’s disclosures, but the document does not explain how they could compel corrections or recover losses.

The ownership restriction matters, too. Partners including Oracle build and own much of the infrastructure reserved for OpenAI, Reuters reported. The company has not said its national compact automatically covers those partner-owned facilities.

The proposed blueprint goes further on electricity costs. “Policymakers should direct state public utility commissions to create large-load tariffs that require large electricity consumers to pay the incremental costs they create,” it says.

A large-load tariff is a special electricity rate for major users such as data centers. The blueprint also urges mandatory reporting, narrow limits on officials’ NDAs and provisions letting governments reclaim tax breaks when developers miss agreed commitments.

The announcement follows a bruising election for the industry. Voters backed restrictions on data centers in multiple states, while candidates pushing for tighter oversight won key races. The results basically send a message to OpenAI and the broader AI industry that communities want a bigger say in how these facilities are built and how their operations affect the people living nearby.

OpenAI says every future campus it owns and operates will receive its own local compact and an annual public progress report.

#5071. Bitcoin Fixes This? Paraguay President Resigns After Bank Lockout Hits 60% of Country

Bitcoin Fixes This? Paraguay President Resigns After Bank Lockout Hits 60% of Country

Paraguayan President Santiago Peña announced his resignation Thursday after the collapse of digital lender Ueno Bank left roughly three million customers unable to access their accounts, turning a banking failure into a political emergency.

The announcement did not end the confrontation. Paraguay’s lower house voted 78-0 to impeach Peña minutes later, sending the charges to the Senate as Vice President Pedro Alliana prepared to take over, per AP.

The bank’s shutdown has also revived an argument familiar to Bitcoin holders: Money held by a financial intermediary is only as accessible as that institution allows. Self-custodied Bitcoin works differently, although it comes with risks of its own.

As the Bitcoin OGs would say, “Bitcoin fixes this.”

Paraguay’s central bank placed Ueno into resolution after regulators found its solvency ratio had fallen below half the legal minimum. The process, announced on November 3, followed accounting adjustments to the bank’s financial statements and left its app, cards, and transfers out of service.

Ueno claimed around 2.9 million customers, more than 60% of Paraguay’s adults. It also held roughly $800 million in public-sector deposits, including pension and health funds, according to AP, complicating an already painful freeze on ordinary savings and wages.

Peña previously held a stake in a company linked to Ueno’s owners, a relationship that fueled allegations of conflicts of interest. He has denied wrongdoing and said he sold his interest in March 2025, according to Reuters.

“My greatest wish is that the return of those funds be guaranteed, that the Deposit Guarantee Fund fulfill its purpose,” Peña said in his resignation address. The Deposit Guarantee Fund would be the equivalent of the FDIC in the United States.

Mensaje del Presidente de la República, Santiago Peña, al pueblo paraguayo. pic.twitter.com/RBz6IfFV9L

— Santiago Peña (@SantiPenap) November 5, 2026

Outside Congress, the anger was less diplomatic. “Santiago Peña and the Colorado Party have stolen all our savings,” protester Luis Pineda told AP. “We are fed up with 70 years of plunder.”

The central bank says Ueno’s failure is an isolated case, not evidence of a broader banking collapse. It has announced a deposit guarantee of up to 228.3 million guaraníes per depositor, roughly $36,000, with initial payments targeting eligible accounts holding no more than 10 million guaraníes.

Bitcoin was born during another banking crisis. Its first block, mined in January 2009, included a newspaper headline about a second bailout for British banks, a historical detail long cited by supporters who distrust reliance on financial intermediaries.

A Bitcoin holder who controls a wallet’s private keys can authorize transfers from the wallet without asking a third party, like a bank, to unlock an account. That is the principle behind the crypto saying “not your keys, not your coins,” and the distinction from leaving Bitcoin on an exchange that can itself freeze withdrawals.

But Bitcoin is not perfect. Self-custody has its own risks: Forget your keys or lose them and there is no way you can recover your funds, there is nobody who can assist you, and no law enforcement agency who can do anything for you.

Paraguay’s central bank said Thursday its first guarantee payments would go to depositors with up to 10 million guaraníes, no Ueno debts and an account at another financial institution.

#5068. This Is Not Your Ordinary Flu Season

The CDC released new data that changes what to expect this season.

EVERY FLU SEASON is different. Some come earlier, others later. Some are mild, while others—like last year—barrel through like a tornado. Every year, scientists do their best to predict what type of influenza virus will hit the US based on data from countries in the Southern Hemisphere whose flu seasons come earlier than ours.

The key word here is predict. Since the influenza virus mutates quickly, it’s hard for scientists to give a 100 percent guarantee that what’s ravaging Australia will also come for the US. That’s what happened in the brutal flu season last year—and this year.

A few weeks ago, scientists gave a forecast of H3N2 (that’s the one dubbed “Super-K”) as the dominant flu strain for the US. But early surveillance in flu cases from the West Coast found that a different type—Influenza A virus subtype H1N1—has overtaken H3N2 as the dominant flu strain in October. The CDC also released new flu data reporting a spike in flu-related hospitalizations.

Does H1N1 sound eerily familiar? H1N1 is also known as swine flu—a strain that sparked a pandemic back in 2009. So far, researchers have linked early flu ER visits to a mutation in H1N1 (a G155E mutation) that changes the way the virus attaches to and infects cells. Past research has found antibodies from past H1N1 infections or vaccinations may not recognize it, especially in younger populations. In other words, there is a chance the mutated influenza virus could evade some pre-existing immunity.

Here is what you need to know about this early jump to flu season and how to best protect yourself against this mutated strain.

HERE’S WHAT THE CDC learned: In mid-October this year, the agency saw 10 times more hospitalizations for flu than it did in 2025 (2.1 hospitalizations per 100,000 people compared to 0.2 per 100,000 during the same week last year).

Among other changes are when and where the virus is spreading. While influenza cases typically tick up around fall, infections began rising in August. The boost of early cases was mostly on the West Coast, including Arizona, Nevada, and California. Hawaii and Louisiana are already at moderate levels. Typically, the US flu season starts in the South.

Flu activity remains low nationally. But as of October 28, the CDC estimates that infections are growing or likely growing in 39 states. The numbers coincide with early reports of flu activity ramping up on the East Coast in states including Pennsylvania and New York.

WHILE SCIENTISTS INITIALLY predicted a “Super-K” flu strain to be dominant, this year’s vaccine accounts for multiple flu strains. The current flu shots will target three strains: H1N1, B/Victoria lineage, and H3N2. It’s too soon to see if the vaccine will fully protect against the mutated H1N1 strain, but it’s still expected to provide some meaningful protection.

“A mismatch is not a black-or-white situation. We may have less immunity, not no immunity,” Andrew Pekosz, PhD, virologist at the Johns Hopkins Bloomberg School of Public Health told TODAY.com. You can also take some reassurance from last year when people who still got the mismatched flu shot were still less likely to have severe illness and be hospitalized than those who were unvaccinated.

ALONG WITH YOUR traditional flu shot, adults ages 50 and over are eligible for a new flu vaccine called mFLUSIVA made with the same mRNA technology as the COVID vaccines. A phase 3 clinical trial on the mFLUSIVA vaccine found it was more effective at preventing illness from influenza virus subtypes A and B than a standard vaccine. Reminder, the dominant strain right now is Influenza A virus subtype H1N1.

Of the 20,179 people injected with the new shot, only 411 developed a lab-confirmed case (2 percent) 14 days after vaccination during flu season last year. Meanwhile, 557 of 20,124 recipients of the standard vaccine got sick with the flu (2.8 percent).

One caveat, however, was that the new flu vaccine has a greater chance of severe side effects. In the clinical trial, people were more likely to report injection site pain, fatigue, headaches, and muscle weakness.

Don’t want a shot? That’s fine! You also have your choice of a nasal flu vaccine a.k.a. FluMist if you’re ages 2 to 49. “We tend to develop less of an immune response in the nose as we get older, which means the nasal spray vaccine doesn’t work as well in older groups,” John Sellick, DO, infectious disease expert and professor of medicine at the University at Buffalo, previously told MH.

Ultimately, time isn’t running out yet to get yourself armed and ready for the US flu season. The influenza virus is fast and mutates easily, but vaccination remains your best option to weather the storm.

Jocelyn Solis-Moreira, MS is the associate health & fitness for Men's Health and has previously written for CNN, Scientific American, Popular Science, and National Geographic before joining the brand. When she's not working, she's doing circus arts or working towards the perfect pull-up.

#5062. Claude Gets Personal Finance—Anthony Pompliano Accuses Anthropic of Copying His Startup

Claude Gets Personal Finance—Anthony Pompliano Accuses Anthropic of Copying His Startup

Anthropic launched a personal finance feature for Claude on Thursday, letting users connect bank accounts and ask the chatbot what they have spent, what bills are due, and how much money they have left.

The announcement came with an accusation from crypto investor Anthony Pompliano, who says Anthropic tried to get inside information from his competing startup, Silvia, months before the rollout.

Claude can then summarize spending, spot duplicate charges or unexpected fees, explain interest payments, and build charts or bill calendars. Anthropic's example shows a user asking where their money went this month, then following up to find out what remains before payday.

"Access is read-only, and Claude can't move, manage, or invest your money," Anthropic wrote on X. "Like the rest of Claude, Personal Finance has no ads or sponsored recommendations," the company added.

The assistant can see balances, credit limits, account names and types, the last four digits of account numbers, and up to 24 months of transactions from accounts users approve. According to Anthropic's documentation, conversations inside the Personal Finance hub are not used to train its models, but account balances may lag and AI-generated figures should be checked against bank records.

Also, how Anthropic tracks its users’ financial data and uses it beyond model training is not completely clear—and this is extremely valuable information for big data companies.

Pompliano, whose Silvia product bills itself as a personal AI chief financial officer, posted a lengthy complaint on X shortly after Claude's announcement. He alleged that Anthropic personnel began approaching Silvia engineers in April, supposedly because they were interested in the startup's growth and computing needs.

"Anthropic was fishing for details on how we built our product and why it was so popular," he wrote. Pompliano also described an alleged steak dinner involving Anthropic representatives, claiming someone told the Silvia team that the meal was on CEO Dario Amodei's tab.

His engineers declined to divulge sensitive information, he claimed, adding that someone at Anthropic warned his team about the upcoming launch days earlier.

"Today, Anthropic launched a Claude wrapper that is masquerading as a personal finance product," Pompliano wrote. He also claimed Silvia's computing costs are roughly 97% lower when using its own models and hardware instead of frontier AI models, which most likely mean they are using a local model, likely finetuned for the startup’s specific purpose.

That said, Anthropic was not the first general-purpose chatbot maker to move into personal finance, either. OpenAI launched similar bank connections for ChatGPT in May, also using Plaid for read-only access to financial information, and Agents like Hermes, Open Claw, or Grok Bot can also do pretty similar tasks given the proper skills, permissions, and instructions.

Anthropic says Personal Finance is a first step and is exploring partnerships with licensed professionals and regulated financial firms. For now, the beta is restricted to U.S. customers on three paid Claude plans.

#5057. 7 Best Sex Blankets for Mess-Free Fun, According to Sex Experts

Women's Health may earn commission from the links on this page, but we only feature products we believe in. Why Trust Us?

Let's face it: Any kind of sex is likely going to involve fluids. And while that's totally normal, nothing kills the mood quite like dealing with that dreaded wet spot on your bed sheets immediately after having sex. There is a way to avoid that annoyance completely, though—I'm talking about sex blankets.

Sex blankets let you get as messy as you want during sex without worrying about the cleanup afterward. Aside from the fact that they help protect your sheets from lube, ejaculate, period blood, and more, they can also make sex even hotter by creating a more relaxed environment. "The more comfortable our environment is, the less things we’re worried about [and] the easier it is to relax and release into pleasure," says Jenn Gunsaullus, PhD, a sociologist, intimacy and relationship coach, and author of From Madness to Mindfulness: Reinventing Sex for Women. "A sex blanket can help make us more comfortable, and reduce any stress about being judged, making a mess, or messing up something nice."

While sex blankets come in a variety of styles and sizes, the best ones have a few characteristics in common. Generally, you'll want to look for a blanket that's waterproof and designed to protect your mattress topper/couch/floor/countertop from any fluids. It's also best to look for a product that's machine-washable and a great size for your needs. Ahead, sex therapists and sex educators unpack the top traits to look for in a sex blanket.

What really sets a sex blanket aside from a regular one is its capacity to reduce mess. So, you'll need a waterproof product, and it'll help if the blanket is also absorbent, says Goody Howard, a Dallas-based sex educator. "The best sex blanket is going to be one that has good absorption, not just something that is waterproof and doesn't allow things to get wet." She recommends looking for keywords like 'charcoal' and 'microfiber' in the product description. This will let you know it's actually absorbent instead of just a plastic-lined piece of fabric, Howard says.

Size is also an important feature to consider. Some sex blankets don’t cover the entirety of a bed, so if you’re looking to cover a lot of surface area, you'll want to opt for a larger blanket like the Liberator Fascinator Throw. On the other hand, a smaller sex blanket, like the Playdrop Sex Mat, might be more travel-friendly and easier to toss in the wash. It all depends on your needs.

If you plan on keeping your sex blanket out in plain sight, you probably don't want it to stick out like a sore thumb. "Keep in mind where the blanket will live in your space so that the color or design matches the area," Howard says. A product that matches your sheets, comforter, or pillows might also make the whole experience of using a sex blanket feel sexier and more natural.

For the past year, Women’s Health editors vetted the bestselling, top-rated sex blankets on Amazon and beyond. After consulting two sex experts, we personally tested each blanket by pouring a cup of water on it and watching how the material responded. We also sent blankets home with our team of anonymous reviewers for further testing, and evaluated thousands of customer reviews and ratings to narrow down the best sex blankets for a wide range of needs and preferences.

So, if you’re interested in trying it out (or looking for an upgrade), see below for the seven best sex blankets you can buy right now, curated with help from sex experts and tested by real users.

Though this is the priciest option on this list, our testers and experts agree that this sex blanket is also the most luxurious and versatile. Not only does it feature soft and plush microvelvet on both sides, but the Liberator Fascinator also comes in multiple colors and sizes—all of which are fully waterproof. Plus, it has a protective inner liner that locks in moisture, ensuring your bed stays spotless and dry.

According to testers, it does take a while to dry out after a wash, but other than that they love this blanket. One even said that it dramatically enhanced their sex life because it's much more romantic than the towels they were using before purchasing this throw blanket.

Literally as soft as sheep's wool, this blanket is perfect for couples who like getting cozy… in more ways than one (wink). The plush micro fleece side of the blanket is waterproof, while the sherpa side is not, so just keep that in mind before laying it down on your bed. The fabric is thicker and warmer than most sex blankets, and it also comes in four sizes and nine colors, so you can customize this comfy throw to your style.

Our tester says that when she's not having sex on this blanket, she loves to get cozy underneath it on her couch or at her desk. It really is a great multipurpose blanket if you're looking for something you can use anywhere at any time. No one will know you use it during sexy time, too!

Sure, this is technically a waterproof blanket for pets, but that's actually what makes it perfect for enjoying sex without worrying about fluids ruining your bed. Plus, this blanket is lightweight enough for easy travel, yet thick enough to keep you cozy and warm. It also comes in nine colors and three sizes, with the smallest (30 x 40 in.) costing less than $20.

Howard is also a fan. "It is a pet blanket, but hear me out: It serves the same purpose, comes in a great variety of colors, and is ridiculously affordable," she says. You would never even know this was made for your furry friend.

This blanket from Catalonia looks and feels like one of those lightweight throws that are used for decor and thrown on the foot of a bed. Available in several neutral colors, it easily doubles as a daily throw, so you never have to get ready because you technically always are. Each blanket is reversible, so if one side gets a little too wet and wild, you can easily flip it to the other.

Our tester says she's never had an issue with this blanket not protecting her sheets from fluids. However, she did note that although it's comfortable to lay on top of, it's a bit too crunchy to lay underneath.

Don't want the bulk of another blanket on your bed? This one is more like a flat sheet, so it can cover a lot of surface area. In fact, the California King size is the largest blanket on this list, so if you have a big bed (or just want to be extra safe), this is an ideal option.

But to be so lightweight, this blanket doesn’t have an absorbency layer. Instead, it’s made of a thin, raincoat-like material so any liquids will pool on top of it. Our testers didn’t mind this feature, but just noted that this might make the blanket better suited for camping sex than indoor intimacy. That said, the material is designed with a silky feel, so it's comfortable enough to use for regular ol’ sleeping, too. Then, whenever the mood strikes, your bed is already set up.

Love the feel of natural fibers? Look no further. This circular sex blanket from Dame is made of ultra-soft cotton with an internal waterproof layer. It also comes in three colors (sage, oat, and copper) and with a travel-ready pouch for easy and discreet storage.

Overall, our testers appreciated the lightweight and airy feel of this material and that it was absorbent, rather than water-resistant. And while some liked the circular shape of this blanket, others would’ve preferred if the blanket were square- or rectangular-shaped like the other options on this list.

For on-the-go intimacy, your best bet is this travel-sized reversible blanket with a waterproof core. As with the Dame Thro, the cotton waffle weave is absorbent, not water-resistant, so liquids won’t pool on top of the blanket. And while it’s not entirely made of natural fibers (it’s 57 percent polyester), our testers liked how soft the material felt. They also liked the vibrant color combination of lilac and orange. Plus, it folds flat into a storage bag, so it’s easy to stash in your backseat for car sex.

The only potential downside is the price. It’s slightly more expensive than other sex blankets—both similar size and larger.

#5056. Zcash's NU7 Upgrade Goes Live, Recycling 60% of Fees to Fund Future Block Rewards

Zcash's NU7 Upgrade Goes Live, Recycling 60% of Fees to Fund Future Block Rewards

"NU7 has successfully activated on mainnet. A new era of Zcash is here," the project's official account tweeted.

NU7 has successfully activated on mainnet. A new era of Zcash is here. $ZEC pic.twitter.com/rPaSFe9h7j

"This is a huge milestone for Shielded Labs," Jason McGee of Shielded Labs, the Zcash development organization behind the mechanism, tweeted ahead of the activation.

The NSM tackles a problem Zcash inherited from Bitcoin: whether, as block subsidies decline with each halving, transaction fees alone will be enough to pay miners to secure the network. Under the mechanism, 60% of transaction fees will be removed from circulation and gradually reissued through future block rewards, while preserving the 21 million ZEC supply cap. Reissuance will begin in 2031, with the recycled funds spread along a smooth curve on top of the existing halving schedule.

The NSM was Shielded Labs' first project and builds on an idea that developer Nate Wilcox proposed years ago, originally called the Zcash Posterity Fund, McGee said. A separate proposal under consideration would require Zcash Community Grants to contribute 50,000 ZEC from its treasury to the NSM.

— Jason McGee 🛡 (@aquietinvestor) November 5, 2026

NU7 also limits how much private transaction activity can fit into each block, reducing the data wallets must scan to find their payments, according to the upgrade specification.

Shielded Labs' original design would have replaced Zcash's halvings with a smooth issuance curve. Community panels supported that change, but coinholders did not, so an alternative design that kept the halvings was developed instead.

"We still think the original design was simpler and better, but we respect the process that led us here," McGee wrote. "It should be difficult to change the protocol, and the process should resist capture by any single organization, group, or influential individual."

The upgrade arrives as Zcash's community debates the future of Zcash Community Grants (ZCG) and its share of the Dev Fund, the slice of block rewards that pays for development.

The five organizations that coordinate Zcash's coinholder voting, Project Tachyon, Shielded Labs, Valar Group, the Zcash Foundation and ZODL, have set out an expedited timeline for proposals and polling, saying "tensions were high" and that a drawn-out debate risked "deepening divisions within the Zcash community."

Proposals are due by November 8, with polls for coinholders and community panels running from November 16 to November 30. If rough consensus emerges, the groups will support a further network upgrade, NU 7.1, to implement it.

#5054. The 6 Best Beard Trimmers for Men in 2026, Tested by Grooming Editors

Our product picks are editor-tested, expert-approved. We may earn a commission through links on our site. Why Trust Us?

Expert-approved trimmers from Panasonic, Bevel, and more.

THE FIRST STEP to a righteous beard? Grow one. Like, don’t touch your facial hair at all for at least six weeks. The second step—once you have enough length—trim it up. The growing part is easy, but once you get to styling, things get a bit more complicated. Whether you want a long, flowing beard or something shorter and cleaner, using the right beard products to keep your facial fur in shape also ensures your whiskers stay strong and healthy. And no tool is more important than a quality beard trimmer.

I’m one of those weird dudes who had a full beard by the time I left middle school, so I’ve been guinea pigging beard trimmers since before I was doing trigonometry—which, to be fair, extends to this day (is that the one with shapes?). In the years since, I have tried out just about every beard style you can think of: long grisly beard, tight stubble, even a goatee for a short period of time after seeing the first Iron Man movie (I have had all photographic evidence either scrubbed from the internet or burnt in a fire, so don’t even try).

Through this all, a beard trimmer was the most consequential tool in my grooming arsenal, with the best options changing with each style. Nothing else can give you a uniform cut or easily shape or fade a beard the way a trimmer can, no matter the beard’s style. But beyond length possibilities, you’ll want to consider your hair type and where you are in your own personal facial hair journey. For example, if your beard is coarse or very thick, you’ll need a more powerful trimmer like the Bevel trimmer or Braun Series 9.

If you’re new to growing a beard, you might want to look on the simpler side to make sure you get the hang of beard trimming before you invest, like with the Philips Norelco OneBlade. And if you’re a beard veteran, you’ll want something more professional-grade (like the Panasonic trimmer) and fly without the protection of guards. Then there are the specifics of each device (including how easy it is to maintain), which will really determine the best beard trimmer for you to invest in.

I’ve never been shy about how much I love Braun’s grooming devices, especially those with Series 9 in the title. So, of course, I love the All-in-One Series 9 Beard Trimmer and its insanely versatile design. Capable of efficiently tackling beards of any length, not to mention body grooming and even head hair, the Series 9 is the most wide-ranging option on this list.

Coming with ten (!) adjustments, it can tackle any beard style you’re going for. Plus, it has adjustments for other hair jobs, including ones as delicate as trimming nose hairs or as bold as a full crew cut.

What Else to Consider: This trimmer is truly an all-in-one device that can be used on hair from head to toe. I don’t typically recommend using the same device on your face as you do on your body for sanitary reasons, but if you’re in a jam or less worried about cross-contamination (you should be, though), this device is capable of handling any beard or body grooming.

If you are dedicated to sticking to one trimmer for all of your grooming needs, I recommend at least committing to attachments for each area to limit the exchange of bacteria, and this kit has more than enough to do so. That being said, while it's a solid option for touch-ups, the Series 9 lacks the power of true hair clippers to become a regular set for at-home haircuts.

Why We Chose It: The Braun Series 9 is essentially a luxury, souped-up version of the King C. Gillette Pro. It features a similar design with an easy-to-use adjustment dial that makes switching between desired lengths a breeze. When it comes to beard trimming, whether short or long, it performs with the best of them, plowing through hair quickly, not to mention gently.

I had no issues with pulling or tugging, even when tackling multiple months of beard growth using this trimmer. I also appreciated the stellar hard-shell case the Series 9 comes with, as well as the slick charging dock that takes up less space than any other charging station I’ve tried—for beard trimmers or otherwise.

Long-Term Results: Braun claims that these blades are meant to last a lifetime without ever being replaced. While I can’t confirm that this is true, I’ve been using the same ones for over a year, and they still feel like new. However, Braun also claims that this tool has a three-hour runtime per charge; I found it lasts more accurately in the two-hour range.

While still impressive, it's not quite the full 180 minutes they advertise. Braun also doesn’t disclose the motor speed of the Series 9 trimmer, which is strange given how strongly I can confirm it is. Regardless, the power behind this trimmer hasn’t lessened at all over a year of regular use.

Read More: Braun All-in-One Series 9 Style Kit Review

The reason I love this Men’s Health Grooming Award-winning trimmer so much is that it’s just so freaking easy to use. The adjustable dial allows you to toggle between a whopping 19 different length settings without switching attachments, allowing for a truly customized beard trimming experience. No matter what beard length or style you're going for, this is your one-stop tool. The powerful motor powers 9,800 cuts per minute, and the stainless steel blades slice through even the thickest whiskers like butter.

What Else to Consider: The design of this trimmer is pretty genius. It takes away the need to switch out attachments frequently (and the need to somehow store multiple attachments). The rotary dial is convenient and ideal for beginners or people who have pretty straightforward beard shapes that don’t need a lot of finessing.

Experienced users who are used to using more traditional attachments may find there is a learning curve to using this razor and may take a bit of time to understand how the numbers on the dials correspond to different lengths. I, personally, like the ease of use and the innovation, but keep in mind that it may take some getting used to.

Why We Chose It: This trimmer received high marks in my testing process for almost every factor. The motor is one of the most powerful I've tried, and it works well on all types of beards, from thick to thin and especially short to medium-length styles. I found that the battery life is long, and I needed to recharge it less frequently than other models.

The single attachment seemed odd at first, but paired with the rotary dial, it is innovative and easy to use—especially if you frequently switch between lengths. The one downside is that, while the trimmer itself is well-designed and easy to hold, it is slightly too large to comfortably fit in my Dopp kit, making it a little hard to travel with if you’re concerned about saving space.

Long-Term Results: As I've continued to use this trimmer, it just keeps getting easier. Once you get over any learning curve that could come from adjusting to the rotary dial instead of traditional length attachments, the single-length attachment is convenient and very fast to use. Even though I've been using this for months consistently, the battery life has not waned, and the motor and blades still feel as strong as they did when I first used it. Overall, I'm happy with the investment.

This trimmer is the latest version of my favorite budget-friendly beard trimmer. It also features an easy-to-use rotary dial that allows you to switch quickly between lengths and has two different length attachments—one for short styles and one for medium to longer styles. Between the attachments and the wheel, you get an incredible 40 possible lengths to choose from at 0.5 mm increments, which means you can potentially get lengths that not many other trimmers can deliver, allowing you a truly customizable experience.

The trimmer itself, while the brand doesn’t disclose the strength of the motor or the type of metal the blades are made from, feels substantial, and it’s powerful enough to deliver a good trim on pretty much any beard, especially at this price point.

What Else to Consider: Apart from the 40 different lengths you can get with this trimmer, there aren’t a lot of other bells and whistles to speak of. It is rechargeable (with the included power cord) and delivers a long battery life (45 days) with a single charge, but a full charge takes several hours, so if you’re going to travel with it, make sure it’s charged completely before you pack it. The plastic attachments can feel a little flimsy, but they still get the job done once they are attached to the trimmer. It also includes a storage bag, which is handy for both storage and travel.

Why We Chose It: Out of all the trimmers I tried at this price point, this one had the best performance, full stop. Also, you can’t argue with the sheer amount of possible length settings, which blows all the other similarly priced options out of the water. The motor and blades aren’t quite as powerful as higher-priced models, but it still performs well, making it a good option for beard newbies and experienced beard trimmers alike.

Long-Term Results: After months of use, I haven’t noticed any wane in the actual performance of the trimmer when it comes to, you know, trimming. The battery does need charging more often as time goes on, but it still holds up well even if you use this every day. I can’t say I use all 40 length options, well, ever, but they’re good to have if I ever want to experiment a bit. Mostly, I just keep it at the same length all the time, and that’s totally fine by me.

This handy trimmer is one of the most versatile options out there. It can do it all—shave, trim, detail, and more. Use the length guards to clean up short beards or perma-stubble looks. The innovative non-irritating shaver can also detail neck and cheek lines, clean up sideburns, and pretty much anything else you could think of doing. Thanks to its innovative signature 360 blade head that resembles more a Swiffer Sweeper than a standard electric razor, it's able to contort to every angle on your face—moving side to side or up and down—without risking any nicks or cuts.

The compact, lightweight design is super travel-friendly—just toss it into your Dopp kit or toiletry bag and forget about it. With a 45-minute rechargeable battery, you might even be able to leave the cord at home. It's completely waterproof, so it's safe for shower use, and each blade lasts up to four months.

The best part? It's less than $40. Due to its rectangular design and ultra-soft blades, this trimmer struggles with length and can’t really handle bigger jobs, so long beard aficionados may want to look elsewhere. But, for guys who like to keep their beard, mustache, or stubble game tight, this is a damn-near-perfect solution.

What Else to Consider: Keep in mind that the OneBlade is a do-it-all device. Yes, it can trim your (shorter) beard, but it can also shave, and you can even use it all over your body. If you do choose to take it below the neck, this is where the extra blade can come in handy. You don’t want to use the same blades on your body as you do on your face, especially if you use it on your body first. Doing so could run the risk of bacterial cross-contamination, and no one wants something like jock itch on their face (it happens!).

Why We Chose It: In terms of sheer versatility, I really stand behind the OneBlade. Okay, so it’s not the best for guys with longer beards, but for the vast majority of guys out there, especially if you rock the perma-stubble or a short beard look, it’s hard to beat (not to mention it also really shaves).

The blades are also multi-directional, meaning they cut on both ends instead of just one like a traditional trimmer, making it easy to use even if you’ve never used an electric shaver or trimmer before. The sleek, simple design is both easy to hold and easy to travel with, which is always a plus for us.

Long-Term Results: I've been using the OneBlade for years (really), and it remains one of my go-to grooming gadgets. I will say that you really do need to replace the blades every so often (the brand recommends every 4 months, though I’ve found they can last a bit longer in the six-month range), but luckily, they’re much easier to replace than your typical trimmer or shaver. Other than replacing the blades, my OneBlade works just the same as it did right out of the box—even years later.

But inside the casing is a powerhouse that plows through hair, even thick beards, easily, thanks to the 7,200 RPM motor and a specially angled blade that helps to lift up hairs and make it easier to cut them.

It uses a standard USB-C cord to charge up, so you don’t have to worry about losing a special cord. That’s a subtle LED indicator to let you know when your charge is getting low, and the charge speed is fast.

If you’re an experienced beard groomer or have a longer beard that you don’t need attachments to trim, this trimmer is for you. It’s really a professional-grade clipper designed for at-home use, so don’t expect length guards. However, in the right hands, this trimmer is a fine, precise tool that does it all. This isn’t technically a beard trimmer—it’s more the kind of trimmer your barber uses to shape up your hairline and those sorts of things. But the reason it’s on this list is that if you have a long beard, many beard trimmer attachments won’t work for you, even the longest ones. Especially because long beards often need to be shaped freehand with a tool like this.

This powerful trimmer can be used as a detailer and is great at line work. Long-Term Results: When I let my beard really grow, I'm constantly reaching back for this trimmer to manage it. The motor stays powerful, and the blade stays sharp even after months of use. I also regularly use it as a detailer. It’s a barber-level tool that creates sharp, crisp lines on our necks and cheeks, and I've found that even if I use a different trimmer to cut and shape my beard, I reach for this to clean up the edges and make sure they’re extra crisp.

Of course, ultimately what beard trimmer is best for you comes down to your personal needs and preferences (which is why this list has six trimmers, not just one). But there are a few things you can look for that are essential for any beard trimmer, no matter what sort of beard you're caring for. Here’s the lowdown on how to shop for the best beard trimmers in 2026.

You want a strong motor with enough juice to shave or cut to the right length cleanly, smoothly, and with a minimal number of passes (more passes make for more skin irritation). In most cases, you get what you pay for here. The most budget-friendly beard trimmers in our roundup (those under $50) all do a decent job, but they often can’t match the raw power of a more premium, $100-plus model.

This is why you’ll notice most budget options tend not to disclose their motor speed, while all the higher-end devices on this list start at either 6,000 rpm (rotations per minute) or 10,000 cuts per minute, depending on which metric they use to detail motor power.

Much like the motor, the blades of a trimmer will dictate how quickly and effectively it cuts through hair. Typically, you want to look for blades made of durable materials like stainless steel or metal that stay sharp for a long time (many trimmers will even have self-sharpening blades, which is a plus for longevity).

You will eventually need to replace the blades, so choosing one with replaceable blades will always be helpful, but blades made from strong materials, like metals, will last longer than those made of plastic.

Cordless trimmers are great for a lot of reasons, which makes them the preferred choice for most bearded gents. But they can’t match the sheer power of traditional, corded models. If you’re opting for the latter, consider models with a long, flexible power cord like Bevel’s. This makes them both easier to store and to maneuver around your face with better precision.

If you’re opting for a cordless model, it’s important to keep its battery life in mind. Knowing how often you’ll need to charge it can mean never needing to stop in the middle of a trim to recharge (believe us, it happens way more often than you’d think). If you can remember to charge your trimmer often, then battery life may not be super important to you, but if you can’t, travel a lot, or just don’t want the hassle, opting for one with a long battery life is important.

Of course, length attachments are important in a beard trimmer as they allow you the freedom to customize the length of your beard with ease (and ensure an even cut). Some beard trimmers include only the most essential tools and attachments, which is fine if you’re a beginner or just need the basics.

But other, more comprehensive kits are made more versatile with the addition of things like guide combs, extra trimmer heads, nose trimmer attachments, and more. Not having these isn’t necessarily a deal-breaker; again, it’s all about what’s right for you.

I barely remember the days before I had a beard, so you could say I’ve been testing trimmers for roughly two decades at this point. However, over the past two years, I’ve consulted with my fellow Men's Health editors and writers on the best-performing beard trimmers and which they prefer. As a grooming editor, I’m constantly testing the latest and greatest beard-trimming products, so I can also confirm that this list is up to date for the best options available in 2026.

Based on this experience, I was able to narrow down our search before I put a number of beard trimmers to the test to evaluate—or reevaluate—their effectiveness, durability, price point, and ease of use. The final guide below represents my honest picks of the six best beard trimmers worth buying in 2026. For some of the beard trimmers, I've also included before-and-after shots of yours truly so you can get a feel for the type of job each can handle.

Whether you’re buying your first beard trimmer or looking to upgrade, these are the half dozen worth investing in.
