#5019. Anthropic IPO Valuation Could Be Slashed in Half to $1T After AI Stock Rout: Report

Anthropic IPO Valuation Could Be Slashed in Half to $1T After AI Stock Rout: Report

Major investors are increasingly doubtful that Anthropic's initial public offering can go ahead at its expected valuation of $1.5 trillion, after a sharp sell-off in AI stocks, The Wall Street Journal reported on Thursday.

Anthropic, which filed last month to list on Nasdaq under the ticker "ANT," has been aiming to go public the week before Thanksgiving and raise up to $100 billion, according to the Journal. But three major investors who plan to take part in the IPO told the paper the valuation will likely need to come down, with one suggesting it could be closer to $1 trillion.

The company could also raise less than planned, perhaps around $80 billion, one investor told the Journal. Another wondered whether Anthropic might delay the offering until market conditions improve. Anthropic executives and advisers are expected to meet in the coming days to discuss the plans, according to the report.

The doubts follow a rout in AI stocks on Thursday, as investors weighed whether Democratic gains in the midterms could bring stricter oversight of data center construction. The S&P 500 closed down 4.4% and the tech-heavy Nasdaq 100 fell 6.3%, both their worst days since April 2025, while the Philadelphia semiconductor index slid 12.3% and Nvidia dropped 9.2%, NBC News reported. Stocks partly recovered on Friday morning: as of 11:21 a.m. ET, the Nasdaq Composite was up 1.4% and Nvidia had gained 3.4%, according to Yahoo Finance.

Democrats are planning bills to shield consumers from rising electricity costs tied to data centers, require independent testing of advanced AI systems and clarify when AI companies can be held liable for harm, Reuters reported, though any legislation would likely face steep odds while Trump remains in the White House. The party also plans closer scrutiny of leading AI companies, including Anthropic, according to the report.

Sen. Bernie Sanders (I-VT) has called for a "national data center moratorium," while Texas governor-elect Gina Hinojosa has pledged to pause data center approvals in a state that is home to more than a quarter of planned U.S. data center capacity, CNBC reported.

A delay to Anthropic's IPO is now an "escalating risk" in the current environment, technology analyst Richard Windsor told The Telegraph. "A postponement would protect against near-term valuation risks and would be more likely if the correction sustains over a longer period," said Nalin Patel, an analyst at PitchBook, according to the paper.

Some investors want to see Anthropic's October results before assuming a cut. The company's annualized revenue run rate topped $80 billion at the end of October, and it had $146.6 billion in cash as of September 30, according to the Journal.

Traders have pushed back their expectations. On Polymarket, the odds that Anthropic lists by November 20 stood at 43% on Friday, while the chance of a listing by the end of the year was 71%. On Kalshi, where traders bet on when Anthropic will officially confirm its IPO, the odds of that happening before December 1 fell 15 points to 59%, while the chance of it happening before January 1 slipped to 68%. On Decrypt Predict, a prediction market developed by Decrypt's parent company, Dastan, traders also put the odds of an Anthropic IPO before 2027 at 71%.

The valuation now in question is already well below earlier expectations. In September, Decrypt reported that a leaked draft filing showed Anthropic was seeking a valuation of around $2 trillion.

Our favorite pair on this list is 100% cotton.

That’s because (while it is super breathable), once it gets wet, it doesn’t dry out as quickly as other materials. Though if you prioritize natural fabrics for your underwear, this is a great pick.

This underwear comes in either a six-pack or a 10-pack, so you get a mega bang for your buck, as the six-pack goes for under $15. It also comes in tons of assorted styles—and the color options are endless.

It’s a bikini-style underwear, so it offers more coverage than a thong, but I love it because I can have these on all day and forget I’m wearing them. They have a higher leg cut than normal, which helps avoid pinching or bunching, even while I’m moving around a lot. I also notice minimal lines under my clothing with this pair, but I wouldn’t quite say it’s invisible.

I've washed this underwear a ton of times and have never noticed any shrinking or a change in quality, which isn't always the case for affordable Amazon underwear. If you're looking for good quality, inexpensive underwear, this pair is it.

Of all the thongs I’ve tried, this pair from On Gossamer checks most of my boxes. It’s the most durable pair on this list thanks to a high amount of nylon in the fabric. Wash after wash, it feels brand new. And, for a synthetic option, it’s extremely soft and comfortable. Additionally, it comes in many different coverage options (think: brief, bikini, high cut, etc), is super stretchy without slacking on shape and compression, and it has a cotton gusset, which is essential for wicking away moisture.

This underwear has plenty of stretch—it can expand super wide but still snaps back for a snug, comfortable fit, thanks to the spandex in the fabric. It comes in sizes XS to XL, but the extra stretch means it can accommodate a bit beyond that, too.To make matters even better, it’s buttery soft, so it literally feels like you’re wearing nothing. And, it looks like that, too—I’ve worn this underwear under super tight compression leggings, and you can’t see any seams.I wear the Heavenly Stretch Thong for everything—from the gym to the office to running errands—because it’s seamless, lightweight, and moisture-wicking. Even when I get sweaty, it stays dry and comfortable, so I never have to worry about dampness or chafing. By the time I leave the gym, it’s usually dry and feels completely weightless. No need to adjust after burpees or squats—it stays put the entire time.I’d recommend going with your normal size for this panty. I tried a pair in a size down, and found that while it definitely fit, it was a bit tight around the hips. My one con? I wish it came in more color options besides just black and tan.Read more: Best Moisture-Wicking Underwear

Even though I’m a thong girl through and through, a bikini provides more coverage and comes in handy when you're lounging at home or during certain times of the month. And this one is a favorite of mine. With a touch of lace and tons of fun colors, it’s anything but grandma-y. Plus, it’s so soft and comfy, I actually want to wear it all day. I’ve even slept in it—it’s full coverage but feels buttery smooth and cozy.

This pair is made from Tencel modal, which is twice as soft as cotton. Compared to cotton, it’s smoother, more lightweight, and resistant to shrinking. And get this: It’s also super breathable, which is hard to find in panties that aren’t made from cotton.

It's sometimes hard to find comfortable bikini-style underwear that doesn't dig into the skin or move around because of the amount of fabric on the hips. This brief fits like a dream—it stays in place without riding up and has the perfect mid-rise cut. While I wouldn’t wear it with tight leggings, it’s seamless under jeans with no visible panty lines. Plus, since it doesn’t shift throughout the day, I never have to worry about adjusting it.

The sizing is also super inclusive with this underwear, as it ranges from XS to 4XL, so you can always find the right size. And don’t even get me started on the color range—there are more than 100 colors and patterns to choose from (though you won’t find them all under one Amazon listing).

Cotton underwear is great for wearing all day because it's a naturally breathable material, says gynecologist and Chief Medical Officer of Bonafide Health Dr. Alyssa Dweck, MS, MD, FACOG. This pair is made from 100% organic Supima cotton, which has longer fibers than regular cotton, making it feel extra soft and smooth against the skin. The fabric also includes a touch of spandex, which adds just the right amount of stretch. That’s why I love to wear it for walks, Pilates, and even high-impact workouts when I want to feel supported, secure, and ready to move.Plus, the material isn’t too thick. Combined with the smooth lace trim, this underwear is invisible under tight leggings and workout shorts. That’s a big win, since I find it difficult to avoid visible panty lines in styles that aren’t thongs.

And while thong and bikini styles tend to get a lot of love for workouts, the French Cut is one of the most underrated styles, IMO. Its high-leg cut makes it easy to move around in since the fabric doesn’t bunch at the hips or feel restrictive. That makes this pair great for all workouts. Plus, I love that the fit sits above the hips while still offering plenty of coverage. They’re also great for low-rise or super-short shorts when you need a pair of undies that won’t show while you’re on the move. The delicate lace trim detailing also adds a feminine touch, making them great for wearing outside the gym too.

Of all the moisture-wicking underwear I’ve tried, the Spanx Fit To You Thong checked all my boxes in terms of fit, materials, comfort, and sweat-fighting abilities. The nylon-based fabric is soft, breathable, and keeps me cool. It dries quickly, even after high-intensity workouts. By the time I leave the gym, I never have to worry about sweaty underwear if I’m running errands afterward.

It stays invisible under leggings, which can be hard to find in underwear, even in thongs. This pair has light compression for a smoothing effect, while the mid-rise style sits right at the waist without peeking over. There’s also no elastic band, so I never have to worry about an uncomfortable digging sensation.

The moisture-wicking cotton gusset stays in place during even my most intense workouts, offering full coverage without shifting or bunching up. I was skeptical about the “one size fits all” claim, but the stretch really impressed me—it hugs the body just right and prevents any VPLs (visible panty lines). The “one size plus” option is a great addition, making the fit more inclusive. However, I’ve noticed that many colors sell out quickly in the “one size fits all” option, while the “one size plus” tends to stay in stock longer.

I rarely go for full coverage undies, but I love this boybrief style from Aerie. It’s so comfortable and lightweight that I find myself reaching for it on a weekly basis. Normally, full-coverage styles like briefs and shorts dig in and are just…noticeably there all day, IYKWIM. Not this pair. The low rise on the hips helps with the barely-there feel, as does the thinner, super breathable construction.

I also love that this underwear is made primarily from cotton (with a bit of elastane for stretch), which helps with that softness and breathability. The material truly feels like butter on the skin. It feels less like full coverage underwear and more like a skimpy short, so I often wear it on its own with a t-shirt when I’m lounging at home. But it also works great under looser bottoms like basketball shorts, sweatpants, or baggy jeans. I wouldn’t wear it with leggings or anything form-fitting on the bottom, because it does cause VPL under tighter clothing.

When you’re on your period, you need underwear that keeps you dry and wicks away moisture. The Knix Super Leakproof Bikini does just that, absorbing up to eight teaspoons (!!) of blood, pee, or sweat. So if you bleed through your tampon, you’ve got a backup—without needing a pad.

Unlike other period underwear I've tested (which uses ugly, puffy padding to prevent leaking), Knix’s design is super discreet. That's because it uses a moisture-wicking nylon exterior with a cotton gusset that absorbs liquid. Some period underwear feels bulky and constricting, but this pair is so thin and comfortable, it’s like wearing nothing at all. The bonded edges also help prevent side leaks, making it great for overnight wear when you want extra protection without a tampon.

And while leakproof underwear might not sound glamorous, trust me—this pair is anything but bulky. The built-in moisture-wicking pad is so thin you barely notice it, even under sheer lace pants (I tested it at my birthday party!). Plus, it’s machine washable and seamless enough to wear under leggings. If you have a heavier flow, Knix offers thicker options, but this pair is perfect for light days or when you need extra backup.

Merino wool is one of the best materials to look for in a pair of workout underwear, and Branwyn's Essential Bikini is my favorite merino wool option. It dries out very quickly, even when I’m super sweaty. I love that I don’t have to worry about wearing these straight from the gym to brunch if I’m in a hurry.

Merino wool as a technical fabric is one of the few natural fibers that actually performs well in activewear. It comes from the coat of sheep and other animals, is naturally antimicrobial, and wicks away and absorbs sweat rather than letting it sit on the skin. If you run hot during your workouts, you can trust this pair of undies to dry quickly.

The nearly seamless construction also means no weird pinching or discomfort. The stretch (from the spandex in the fabric) means it moves in every direction without feeling restrictive. In fact, this is the most flexible underwear I’ve tested during my runs. Each piece is dyed in small batches, making every pair unique.

This pair from lululemon is perfect for all of that. It looks just like a pair of biker shorts (so no one will know it’s underwear!), but it has the breathability and moisture-wicking benefits of a great pair of undies.

It feels buttery soft, but the bit of spandex gives it a subtle snap, making it great for workouts or light compression under a dress. The flat seams stay invisible under clothes and help prevent chafing, while the smooth waistband sits comfortably without digging in or showing through.

This short has a five-inch inseam and a super high rise, making it a versatile fit for all kinds of outfits. In my opinion, it’s the perfect length for layering under clothes. #5014. Meghan Trainor. Zedd. Joe Jonas and Sophie Turner. This $18 Million Manse Is Collecting Celeb Owners. Who’s Next?

Meghan Trainor has listed her Encino, California, home for just under $18 million. If the songstress and her actor husband, Daryl Sabara, pass the torch to another notable name, it would continue the estate’s ongoing chain of celebrity stewards. Trainor bought the house from DJ Zedd in 2024 for about $17 million; Zedd had paid $15.2 million in 2021 when he bought it from Joe Jonas and Sophie Turner, who were the first to move into the six-bedroom, 12-bathroom manse when it was completed in 2019. They scooped it up for $14.1 million. To put it simply: The property has never been owned by someone without a Wikipedia page. As its listing description reads: “This compound is for the most discerning Buyer.” Unsurprisingly, the home hosts a swath of amenities that are as elite as its residents.

Tucked behind tall gates and hedges, the contemporary-style build, designed by Jae Omar, spans nearly 15,000 square feet. The dwelling’s main floor hosts a great room with two fireplaces, wall-to-wall pocket doors that open to the backyard, and a library nook. The eat-in kitchen is complete with a built-in bar and a sprawling center island with a live-edge wooden breakfast bar seating seven. An additional catering kitchen, an office, and an impressive playroom outfitted with a climbing wall, a slide, and a foam pit round out the main level, along with a fitness and wellness center and “perhaps the best-sounding studio in the world,” according to Zedd, who added that amenity during his ownership.

Five ensuite bedrooms are situated upstairs, as well as another fireplace-warmed living area. The primary suite boasts yet another fireplace, a wet bar, a bathroom, two spacious dressing rooms, and walls that slide open to a sizable balcony. Outdoor amenities include a pool and spa, a kitchen, a putting green, a projection screen, and a fire pit. A guesthouse with a billiards pub and a movie theater round out the compound. Watch this space to see if some lucky celeb buyer keeps the home’s hot streak alive.
